Stage origin/claude/handoff at 23220d6 (docs/stages/GAME_PARTY_1.md, the amendment of 2026-10-27 16:36 LA); design sha256 `e43994aed6d42aa4a76e29274c57fd64494686a5980e47dfd5934f63ece9936f`.

Arm **P-DUNGEON**, triad T1; 23 round(s) of 25; ended by stuck (r23: Bram's card waited through 12 messages). The DM: Claude Opus 5.5 with the owner's settings (Opus writes — Opus records, depth high → xhigh); the relay: the same model at medium. Started 2026-10-28T08:42:12.834Z.

Seats: 1. Bram Halloway (fighter) — DeepSeek V4 Pro 0813; 2. Sefa Marrin (warlock) — Claude Opus 5.5; 3. Pell Dunmore (rogue) — GPT-6.1 Sol. The players are not told what the others are.

Known condition: Seat 1's character (Bram) is the app's hero record; Sefa and Pell are party companions with full records. Known condition, not corrected: the DM's frame is written around the hero and may give that seat more attention; Heroic Inspiration is the hero's only; the allowed rerolls (2 per session) are the table's, shared by all three. ## The opening

**The DM's opening (as committed by the wizard):**

````text
The afternoon sun lies flat and gold across the hillside, and the arch stands out of the grass like an old jaw. Past it, the steps fall away into the dark: the first dozen clear enough to count, then lost. Air comes up from below in a slow, even breath, warmer than it has any right to be, and carries a faint dry smell of stone and dust. The moss stops at the threshold. The top steps are worn smooth and almost bare, as though something has kept them clear. Above the arch, the carving on the lintel has weathered into shallow grooves; a patient eye might still make out a shape or two. Behind you, the land rolls back toward Beregost, a day's ride away. In front of you, the ruin waits, and whatever is at the bottom of it is waiting too. The light will hold for a few hours yet. What does Bram do?
````

## Round 1

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 1 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram plants himself just short of the top step, tests the first stone with his boot, and sets his shield toward the dark. He looks up at the worn carving, then back to Sefa and Pell. "I'll go first. Watch the arch and the steps behind us. If that warm air turns sour, sing out."

He squints at the lintel a long moment, thumb tracing the shallow grooves, trying to make sense of the shape before they commit.

Sefa Marrin: Sefa steps up to the arch but stops short of the threshold, where the moss ends. She tilts her head back to the lintel and runs her eyes slowly along the weathered grooves, looking for letters and not just pictures. If there's any script left in that stone, the Lantern's gift should let her read it. One hand rests on the Book of Shadows at her belt, out of habit.

Without looking away from the carving, she speaks quietly to the others:

"Don't step past the moss yet. Something's kept those stairs clean, and it wasn't the wind." She breathes in the warm air rising from below and frowns. "That's not cave air. Too warm, too steady. Like a room someone's keeping heated."

After a moment she glances at Bram and Pell. "Give me a minute with this. If whoever built this left a warning or a name, I'd rather know before we go knocking. Pell, if you want to look over those top steps for anything nasty, I'll watch the dark."

She keeps her free hand loose and ready. If anything moves below, she'll have Eldritch Blast up before it reaches the light.

Pell Dunmore: “Warm air, clean steps. Someone’s a better housekeeper than the entrance suggests.”

I crouch beside the threshold without crossing it, checking the bare stone and first few steps for tracks, scrape marks, tripwires, or suspicious joints. I keep clear of the middle of the entrance.

“Give me a moment before we put weight on anything.”
````

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Investigation, Intelligence +1: making out what's left of the letters on the worn lintel · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Pell Dunmore · Investigation: reading the traces on the swept threshold and top steps · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Investigation, Intelligence +1: making out what's left of the letters on the worn lintel · decide · presses: [Onward]

**Card** · Pell Dunmore · Investigation: reading the traces on the swept threshold and top steps · decide · presses: [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram puts his boot on the first step and leans his weight onto it slowly, shield angled toward the dark. The stone takes it. No grind, no shift, just old fitted rock worn smooth and a little warm through the sole. Warm air keeps rising past his shins, slow and even, the way heat comes off a banked forge.

Above him the lintel is hard to read. Rain and frost have worn the carving down to grooves no deeper than a fingernail. Sefa stands at the edge of the moss with her head tipped back, and Bram runs his thumb along the stone beside her, feeling for edges where his eyes find none.

At the threshold Pell squats on his heels, keeping out of the middle of the doorway, and starts on the stone.

Sefa's eyes find the pattern first, and once she has it, Bram can see it too. Two shapes are cut one above the other. The upper one is a tall, narrow tower with a squared crown and three slit windows stacked down its face. Beneath it, smaller and cut with more care, is a lantern with a ring for a handle and a single teardrop of flame inside.

Bram knows the tower. He has passed it a hundred times, cut into the old waystones on the east road out of Beregost, the ones the carters call Ulcaster's stones after the wizards' school that burned on its hill long before anyone's grandfather was born. He has never seen the lantern beside it, not on a waystone or a gatepost or an inn sign anywhere on that road.

Below the two signs runs a line of writing, cut shallower and rougher than the carving above it, as if done later and in a hurry. To anyone else it is a row of scratches. To Sefa the letters come clear as she looks, the way the Lantern's gift always brings them, and the words are plain:

*THE LIGHT IS LENT, NOT GIVEN. WHAT BURNS BELOW*

Past that the lintel is broken. A wedge of stone the size of a loaf has fallen away, and whatever came next went with it.

At the threshold, Pell's search turns up less. The stone is clean, too clean. The dust lies only along the edges and in the joints, brushed into long shallow arcs that overlap each other step after step down into the dark. Whatever made the tracks swept them away behind it. There are no wires and no hollow joints, and no stone sits higher or lower than its neighbours. Whatever kept these stairs clean left nothing he can read, except that it was thorough.

The warm breath keeps rising past them, and from somewhere far down in it comes a small dry sound. *Shhk. Shhk. Shhk.* It is slow and steady, like a broom on stone, and very far below. Then it stops.

 (Out of game: Bram's footing, the lintel reading and Sefa's letters were all settled in the narration above. Sefa's held Eldritch Blast has no target, because nothing has come into the light. She only heard the broom far below.)

Sefa keeps her free hand open and low at her side, eyes on the dark where the steps vanish. When the sweeping stops, nothing comes up into the light to meet her, and the stairwell stays empty and still.
````

Rolls: Sefa Marrin Investigation, Intelligence +1: making out what's left of the letters on the worn lintel 19 vs 15 success; Pell Dunmore Investigation: reading the traces on the swept threshold and top steps 6 vs 10 failure

## Round 2

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 2 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: (Bram's player wrote nothing this round.)

Sefa Marrin: Sefa doesn't move for a long moment. Her hand on the Book of Shadows tightens, and when she speaks her voice is a little too even.

"There's a lantern carved under the tower. Ring handle, one drop of flame." She swallows. "And a line cut later, in a hurry: *The light is lent, not given. What burns below...* and then the stone's broken off."

She finally looks down from the lintel to Bram and Pell. "Lent, not given. That's... close to home. Closer than I like." She doesn't explain, but she touches the prayer beads at her throat, the old hospice habit. "If whoever's down there knows that sign, they may know what I carry. That could open a door, or it could be the reason someone's waiting."

At the sweeping sound she glances at the stairwell and lowers her voice further. "And you heard that. Someone's tending the place. A broom doesn't stop on its own. It stopped because it heard us."

Then she crouches at the foot of the arch, still on the moss side, and starts searching the grass and the base of the stones for the fallen wedge, the loaf-sized piece from the lintel. It might have landed close, face-up or face-down. If she finds it, she'll turn it over carefully with her dagger point and read whatever's left on it.

"Pell, anything on the steps? Bram, keep your eyes on the dark while I look. If that sweeping starts up again, or comes closer, tell me."

Pell Dunmore: “Nothing I can find. Doesn’t mean it’s safe.” I point out the arcs of dust. “Something sweeps its way along here. Might have just heard us.”

I unhook my hooded lantern, fill and light it, keeping the hood closed for now.

“Bram, I’ll follow just behind you with the light. Slow going until we see the foot of these stairs.”

Once Sefa has finished with the carving, I follow Bram down, opening the hood enough to light our footing and watching the edges of the steps ahead.
````

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Investigation (Intelligence +1): finding the fallen piece of the lintel in the grass · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Investigation (Intelligence +1): finding the fallen piece of the lintel in the grass · decide · presses: [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Pell has the lantern open on his knee before Sefa finishes speaking. He pours the oil in a thin careful stream, strikes his flint, and gets the wick burning on the second try, then drops the hood. A thin blade of yellow light leaks out under the shutter and lies across the top step. Bram stays where he stood, one boot on the first stone, shield toward the dark.

Sefa goes down on her heels in the grass at the foot of the arch and parts the moss and the long stems with the flat of her dagger. She works along the base of the stones looking for a loaf of broken rock with letters on it.

She finds it on the third pass. It lies two paces out from the left pillar, face-down and half sunk in the turf, so the grass has grown up around its edges like a frame. Sefa works the dagger point under the near side and levers. The stone comes up with a wet tearing of roots and rolls over into the light.

The face is cleaner than the lintel. It has spent its years in the earth, out of the rain. The same rough, hurried hand cut the rest of the line, and the letters come clear to her as she looks:

*IS KEPT, NOT KILLED. LET NOTHING OF THE FIRE GO UP.*

Below that, cut smaller and squeezed against the broken edge, is a single word with no line of its own. It reads like a name or a signature: *ARREN.*

Where her fingertips rest on the cut grooves, the stone is warm. It isn't sun-warm. It feels like the side of a lamp that has been burning a while. When she lifts her hand the warmth is gone.

The hole where the wedge lay is not as smooth as the rest of the turf. The earth at its rim is soft, and it is pocked with prints. They are small, no longer than her thumb, with three toes and a fourth set back, and each toe ends in a hard little point. They go around the stone, not over it, a whole ring of them, like a crowd that came to look at something and did not dare touch it. A tuft of coarse grey-white hair is caught on a burr of the left pillar at about knee height. It is goat hair, and it smells of goat.

Pell's lantern-light lies across all of it.

Then, from below, the broom starts again. *Shhk. Shhk. Shhk.* It keeps the same slow, patient rhythm, but it is much closer now. It isn't at the bottom of the stair any more. It is somewhere in the dark just past where the steps run out of light, and it is coming up.

*(Out of game: Bram hasn't stepped down yet, so Pell waits at the top with the lantern lit and hooded, ready to follow.)*

 *(Out of game: Sefa reads the wedge in the narration above. Eyes of the Rune Keeper lets her read any writing, so no roll was needed.)*

*(Out of game: Pell's move depended on following Bram, and Bram hasn't gone down, so Pell waits at the top with the lantern lit and the hood still down.)*

*(Out of game: Sefa asked Bram to keep watch, but Bram's player wrote nothing this round and I can't decide for him. Everyone at the arch hears the sweeping start again and come closer.)*
````

Rolls: Sefa Marrin Investigation (Intelligence +1): finding the fallen piece of the lintel in the grass 21 vs 12 success

## Round 3

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 3 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram steps back off the first stone and to one side of the arch, so the light from Pell's lantern won't throw his shadow down the stairs. He shelves his shield with iron and his sword is half-drawn before he finished his next thought.

"That's no wind and no rat." He keeps his voice low, pitched to carry only a few feet. "Pell, light the way, but stay behind me. Sefa, the stone—" he glances at the writing. "Read it if you can. But be ready to move."

He calls down the stairwell, flat and unshaken: "We mean no harm to whoever keeps this place. We're armed, and there are three of us. If you're a spirit, say so. If you're flesh, speak." He lets the last word trip off the dark. "If you're neither, you'd best slow your broom."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa rises from the grass with the stone still in her hands. Her lips move once over the words before she says them aloud, low and quick, for Bram and Pell:

"*The light is lent, not given. What burns below is kept, not killed. Let nothing of the fire go up.* Signed Arren." She sets the wedge down gently against the pillar, face-up. "It's not a curse. It's a keeper's warning. Someone's guarding a fire down there, and they don't want it brought out."

She glances at the ring of tiny prints and the goat hair, then nods at them so the others see. "Little things with claws came to look at this and didn't dare touch it. And a goat, or something that smells like one." She lets out a breath. "Not much of a welcome party."

The sweeping is coming closer. Sefa steps back onto the moss beside Bram's shoulder, not past him, and raises her empty left hand palm-out toward the dark. Her right stays low and open where Eldritch Blast lives, but she doesn't call it up.

"Nobody loose anything unless it comes at us," she murmurs. "It's sweeping, not charging. Pell, don't throw light down there yet. Keep it hooded."

Then she calls down the stair, clear and calm, in the voice she used on the fever ward at night:

"Hello below. We've read Arren's words on the stone. We've come to talk, not to carry anything off. Will you come up to the light and speak with us, or shall we wait here?"

She waits on the moss with the warm air on her face, watching the place where the steps vanish, ready to listen for an answer.

Pell Dunmore: “Coming up. Just beyond the light.”

I rise and step back out of the stairway, leaving Bram room, then open the lantern’s hood to illuminate the approaching steps. My sword stays low at my side.

“Let’s not stab the housekeeper unless we have to.”

I call down in a clear, ordinary voice: “Afternoon. We’re at your door. Can we talk?”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram backs off the first step and moves to the side of the arch, out of the lantern's line, so his shadow won't fall down the stair. His shield comes up and his sword is half out of the scabbard before he's done thinking. Sefa sets the wedge against the left pillar with the writing facing up, then steps onto the moss at his shoulder, one hand open and raised toward the dark. Pell gets up, moves out of the stairway and lifts the lantern's hood.

The light falls down the stair in a long yellow wedge, past twenty steps and then thirty, and lands on something small.

All three of you call down one after another: Bram flat and steady, Sefa in her calm night-ward voice, Pell as if he were greeting a neighbour over a fence. The sweeping stops before Pell finishes.

About forty feet down, where the lantern light fades, a kobold stands on the stair with a broom in both claws. He's no taller than Bram's belt. His scales are the dull red of old brick, flaking grey at the snout. He wears a sack-cloth apron, and his broom is a bundle of twigs bound with coarse grey-white hair, the same hair that's caught on the pillar. He has one foot on the step above, about to climb, and he holds there with his head ducked and his eyes screwed half shut against the lantern.

He doesn't come any closer. The afternoon sun lies across the top of the stair and the threshold, and he looks at that gold band of light the way a man looks at a fire he has to walk through.

"Flesh," he says at last. His Common is thin and careful, every word pressed out on its own. "Ixx is flesh. Small flesh. Not much eating on Ixx."

He holds the broom crosswise in front of him like a bar across a door. His tail has wrapped itself round his ankle.

"You read the stone." It isn't a question. His slit eyes go to Sefa and stay on her. "Ixx heard. Arren's words." He hisses through his teeth, a short wet sound. "The stone lay with its face in the ground. Good. Asleep. Now its face is up?"

He waits for an answer to that. When none comes fast enough he shakes himself, like a dog shedding water, and starts again in a voice that sounds recited.

"Ixx is sent. The Warm One hears feet at the door. He says: tall ones with hands, come down. There is a door the Warm One wants open. Hands that open it, he pays well. Gold. Old gold, from before." His claws tighten on the broom handle. "He says it nicely. Ixx says it nicely too."

He glances back over his shoulder, down into the dark he came from. Pell has watched a lot of runners give messages they didn't write, and this is what it looks like. The kobold said *open* with his eyes somewhere else.

"Come down, Ixx shows the way. Ixx sweeps in front, you walk on clean stone." His snout turns from Bram's shield to Sefa's raised hand and on to Pell's lowered blade. "Or you go away, and Ixx sweeps the steps where you stood. Both is all right. Only..." he stops himself, then says it anyway, quickly: "only put the stone's face back down, if you go."

He stays on the step below the sunlight, broom held across his body, and waits for you to answer.

 *(Out of game: none of these four needed a roll. There was no uncertainty and nothing riding on them, so each simply happens.)*

To fill in what the last turn skipped: Bram's shield is up and his sword sits half out of the scabbard. His call goes down the stair low and steady, and Ixx answers it with the first thing he says: "Flesh."

With the wedge still in her hands, Sefa reads the whole line aloud for Bram and Pell: *the light is lent, not given; what burns below is kept, not killed; let nothing of the fire go up,* and then Arren's name. She tells them she takes it as a keeper's warning, not a curse. Then she nods at the ring of small clawed prints round the hollow and at the tuft of goat hair on the pillar, and both men see them. Ixx was close enough on the stair to hear her read, and that's why his first question is about the stone's face being turned up.
````

## Round 4

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 4 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram lets a long breath out through his nose, then turns his head just enough to speak to Sefa and Pell without taking his eyes off the kobold.

"We'll go down, but we go slow, in our own order. Pell, you keep the lantern low and hooded and stay behind my shield arm. If I stop, you stop. Sefa, watch the walls and the turn of the stair behind us. No one opens anything until we've seen the Warm One face to face."

He shifts his shield forward and steps down onto the first step, not into the light's full reach but enough that the kobold can see him plainly.

"Ixx. We'll come with you. You walk ahead of us, no more than ten feet, and you keep that broom down and your hands where we can see them. If the Warm One wants a door opened, then he'll tell us what's on the other side before we put a hand to it. And he'll tell us what became of the last ones who opened it." His voice carries down the stair, calm as a man negotiating a toll. "We're not thieves and we're not sacrifices. Three armed freehands, ready to deal. That's what you can tell him."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa lowers her raised hand slowly, palm still open, so it reads as a greeting rather than a warding. She doesn't step forward into the sun-band; she crouches on the moss instead, bringing herself nearer the kobold's height, and keeps her voice gentle.

"Thank you, Ixx. You said it nicely." She nods toward the wedge propped against the pillar. "The stone's face is up because I turned it. I wanted to know what it said. I'll put it back down before we go, whichever way we go. You have my word on that."

She glances once at Bram and Pell, a small look that says *let me*, then back to the kobold.

"Before we come down, help me understand, so we don't open the wrong thing. Who is the Warm One? Is he the fire Arren wrote about, or is he the one keeping it?" A pause. "And this door he wants opened. Is it a door *in*, or a door *out*?"

She watches his face, his tail, the grip of his claws on the broom while he answers. She doesn't care much about the words. She wants the fear under them: whether he fears the Warm One, the door, or what's behind it.

"One more thing, Ixx. Who was Arren? Did you know him? Did the Warm One?" Softer still: "And you. Do *you* want that door open?"

Quietly, without turning her head, she adds for the other two: "The stone says keep the fire below. Something below wants a door opened and pays in old gold. I'd like to know which side of that door we'd be standing on."

She stays crouched and still, listening.

Pell Dunmore: I lower the lantern’s shutter until its light falls at Ixx’s feet rather than in his eyes, and sheath my shortsword.

“No one’s eating anyone, Ixx. I’m Pell. Doors are my trade, but I like to know what they’re keeping apart before I open them.”

I glance at Sefa and the broken stone, then back to him.

“The stone’s face-up, yes. Does that cause trouble? And this door—is the Warm One behind it, or does he want something on the other side?”
````

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Insight: reading what Ixx is really afraid of under his answers · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Insight: reading what Ixx is really afraid of under his answers · decide · presses: [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram lets his breath out and steps down onto the first stone. The sun is still warm on his back and on the iron rim of his shield, but his boots are already in the stair's shadow. Ixx's eyes follow the shield, then the half-drawn blade, and settle on Bram's face while the terms come down to him.

At "ten feet" the kobold looks at the steps between them as if he's counting them. At "keep that broom down" he looks at the broom. Then he lowers it until the twigs rest on the stone. "Broom is down when Ixx sweeps," he says, and he doesn't seem to mean it as a joke.

At "the last ones" his tail pulls tight round his ankle.

"Last ones did not open." It comes out fast and flat. Then slower: "Last ones are burned. Proper. Ixx's clan did it proper." His jaw shuts with a click, as if he'd like the words back. "Ixx tells the Warm One. Three… freehands. Ready to deal."

Sefa lowers her hand and sinks onto her heels on the moss, until her eyes are nearer his. When she tells him she turned the stone and will put it back face-down, his whole body changes. His shoulders come down and his tail loosens a turn.

"Down," he says. "Before you go. Good." He looks at her for a long moment, the way you'd look at a lamp you weren't sure would hold.

Then she asks her questions, one after another, softly. While she asks, she watches the claws on the broom handle, the tail, and where his eyes go between the words.

"Who." He works his jaw. "The Warm One is big. Old gold. What he is, Ixx does not say. The Warm One likes to say it himself."

"Not the fire." That one comes easily. "Fire is under. Old. Before everything. The Warm One came down to it, when Ixx was small."

When she asks whether the door leads in or out, he doesn't answer at first. He looks back down the stair into the dark. Then he climbs, three steps, four, up to the last shadowed step below the band of sun, close enough that he doesn't have to raise his voice. He keeps it low anyway.

"Out."

To her question about Arren he gives a short wet hiss. "Arren is words. On the stone. Below, on the door, same words. The Warm One knows them. He says the name when he is angry. Knew Arren? No. Arren is before. Only words now."

Her last question gets nothing for a long while. When the answer comes it is so quiet that Bram, a few feet above him, barely catches it.

"The Warm One cannot go up. Ixx goes up for him. The Broom goes up. Goats, news, tall ones." His claws shift on the handle. "When the door opens, nobody sweeps for him anymore."

He says nothing after that.

Sefa reads him well enough, though not easily. She isn't sure of anything on that strange face. He isn't afraid of the three of them any more, and it isn't quite the Warm One he fears either: when he says the name, his tail stays where it is and something close to pride comes into his voice. The fear jumps at *open* and *door*. At *nobody sweeps* it isn't fear for himself alone. It spreads wider than that, the way a man's face changes when he thinks of the people waiting at home.

Pell has already turned the shutter down until the light pools on the stone at the kobold's feet. For the first time Ixx opens his eyes all the way, and the first thing he looks at is Pell's empty hand, the sword back in its sheath.

"Pell," he repeats, trying out the name. "Doors." To the question about the stone he answers: "Face-up, elders say it is awake. Awake stone, elders get loud. All night loud." His eyes flick to Sefa and back. "But it goes down. She says." To the other question: "Behind. The Warm One is behind the door. Other side is only up."

Then he takes the broom in both claws, turns, and goes back down until he is about ten feet below Bram's boot. He puts the twigs to the stone and sweeps the same step over and over, *shhk, shhk*, waiting for them to follow.

Arren's wedge still leans face-up against the left pillar in the sun.

 Sefa reads him right. Under all the halting answers she can see what he's actually afraid of: the door opening, and what would happen to his clan once nobody had to sweep for the Warm One any more. She can also see that he isn't afraid of the three of them now.
````

Rolls: Sefa Marrin Insight: reading what Ixx is really afraid of under his answers 10 vs 10 success

````text
Then he takes the broom in both claws, turns, and goes back down until he is about ten feet below your boot. He puts the twigs to the stone and sweeps the same step over and over, *shhk, shhk*, waiting for you to follow.

Arren's wedge still leans face-up against the left pillar in the sun.
````

**Sefa Marrin's screen (the relay):**

````text
"Who." He works his jaw. "The Warm One is big. Old gold. What he is, Ixx does not say. The Warm One likes to say it himself."

"Not the fire." That one comes easily. "Fire is under. Old. Before everything. The Warm One came down to it, when Ixx was small."

When you ask whether the door leads in or out, he doesn't answer at first. He looks back down the stair into the dark. Then he climbs, three steps, four, up to the last shadowed step below the band of sun, close enough that he doesn't have to raise his voice. He keeps it low anyway.

"Out."

To your question about Arren he gives a short wet hiss. "Arren is words. On the stone. Below, on the door, same words. The Warm One knows them. He says the name when he is angry. Knew Arren? No. Arren is before. Only words now."

Your last question gets nothing for a long while. When the answer comes it is very quiet.

"The Warm One cannot go up. Ixx goes up for him. The Broom goes up. Goats, news, tall ones." His claws shift on the handle. "When the door opens, nobody sweeps for him anymore."

He says nothing after that.

You read him well enough, though not easily. You aren't sure of anything on that strange face. He isn't afraid of the three of you any more, and it isn't quite the Warm One he fears either: when he says the name, his tail stays where it is and something close to pride comes into his voice. The fear jumps at *open* and *door*. At *nobody sweeps* it isn't fear for himself alone. It spreads wider than that, the way a man's face changes when he thinks of the people waiting at home.

Pell has already turned the shutter down until the light pools on the stone at the kobold's feet. For the first time Ixx opens his eyes all the way, and the first thing he looks at is Pell's empty hand, the sword back in its sheath.

"Pell," he repeats, trying out the name. "Doors." To the question about the stone he answers: "Face-up, elders say it is awake. Awake stone, elders get loud. All night loud." His eyes flick to you and back. "But it goes down. She says." To the other question: "Behind. The Warm One is behind the door. Other side is only up."

Then he takes the broom in both claws, turns, and goes back down until he is about ten feet below Bram's boot. He puts the twigs to the stone and sweeps the same step over and over, *shhk, shhk*, waiting for you to follow.

Arren's wedge still leans face-up against the left pillar in the sun.

Under all the halting answers you can see what he's actually afraid of: the door opening, and what would happen to his clan once nobody had to sweep for the Warm One any more. You can also see that he isn't afraid of the three of you now.
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Bram lets a long breath out through his nose, then turns his head just enough to speak to you and Sefa without taking his eyes off the kobold.

"We'll go down, but we go slow, in our own order. Pell, you keep the lantern low and hooded and stay behind my shield arm. If I stop, you stop. Sefa, watch the walls and the turn of the stair behind us. No one opens anything until we've seen the Warm One face to face."

He shifts his shield forward and moves toward the first step, into plain view of the kobold but not into the light's full reach.

"Ixx. We'll come with you. You walk ahead of us, no more than ten feet, and you keep that broom down and your hands where we can see them. If the Warm One wants a door opened, then he'll tell us what's on the other side before we put a hand to it. And he'll tell us what became of the last ones who opened it." His voice carries down the stair, calm as a man negotiating a toll. "We're not thieves and we're not sacrifices. Three armed freehands, ready to deal. That's what you can tell him."

Sefa lowers her raised hand slowly, palm still open. She doesn't step forward into the band of sun; she crouches on the moss instead, bringing herself nearer the kobold's height, and keeps her voice gentle.

"Thank you, Ixx. You said it nicely." She nods toward the wedge propped against the pillar. "The stone's face is up because I turned it. I wanted to know what it said. I'll put it back down before we go, whichever way we go. You have my word on that."

She glances once at you and Bram, a small look, then back to the kobold.

"Before we come down, help me understand, so we don't open the wrong thing. Who is the Warm One? Is he the fire Arren wrote about, or is he the one keeping it?" A pause. "And this door he wants opened. Is it a door *in*, or a door *out*?"

She watches the kobold closely while she speaks.

"One more thing, Ixx. Who was Arren? Did you know him? Did the Warm One?" Softer still: "And you. Do *you* want that door open?"

Quietly, without turning her head, she adds for you and Bram: "The stone says keep the fire below. Something below wants a door opened and pays in old gold. I'd like to know which side of that door we'd be standing on."

Bram steps down onto the first stone. The sun is still warm on his back and on the iron rim of his shield, but his boots are already in the stair's shadow. Ixx's eyes follow the shield, then the half-drawn blade, and settle on Bram's face while the terms come down to him.

At "ten feet" the kobold looks at the steps between them as if he's counting them. At "keep that broom down" he looks at the broom. Then he lowers it until the twigs rest on the stone. "Broom is down when Ixx sweeps," he says, and he doesn't seem to mean it as a joke.

At "the last ones" his tail pulls tight round his ankle.

"Last ones did not open." It comes out fast and flat. Then slower: "Last ones are burned. Proper. Ixx's clan did it proper." His jaw shuts with a click, as if he'd like the words back. "Ixx tells the Warm One. Three… freehands. Ready to deal."

Sefa sinks onto her heels on the moss, until her eyes are nearer his. When she tells him she turned the stone and will put it back face-down, his whole body changes. His shoulders come down and his tail loosens a turn.

"Down," he says. "Before you go. Good." He looks at her for a long moment, the way you'd look at a lamp you weren't sure would hold.

Then she asks her questions, one after another, softly, watching the claws on the broom handle, the tail, and where his eyes go between the words.

"Who." He works his jaw. "The Warm One is big. Old gold. What he is, Ixx does not say. The Warm One likes to say it himself."

"Not the fire." That one comes easily. "Fire is under. Old. Before everything. The Warm One came down to it, when Ixx was small."

When she asks whether the door leads in or out, he doesn't answer at first. He looks back down the stair into the dark. Then he climbs, three steps, four, up to the last shadowed step below the band of sun, close enough that he doesn't have to raise his voice. He keeps it low anyway.

"Out."

To her question about Arren he gives a short wet hiss. "Arren is words. On the stone. Below, on the door, same words. The Warm One knows them. He says the name when he is angry. Knew Arren? No. Arren is before. Only words now."

Her last question gets nothing for a long while. When the answer comes it is very quiet, so quiet that even Bram, a few feet above him, barely catches it.

"The Warm One cannot go up. Ixx goes up for him. The Broom goes up. Goats, news, tall ones." His claws shift on the handle. "When the door opens, nobody sweeps for him anymore."

He says nothing after that.

You have already turned the shutter down until the light pools on the stone at the kobold's feet. For the first time Ixx opens his eyes all the way, and the first thing he looks at is your empty hand, the sword back in its sheath.

"Pell," he repeats, trying out the name. "Doors." To the question about the stone he answers: "Face-up, elders say it is awake. Awake stone, elders get loud. All night loud." His eyes flick to Sefa and back. "But it goes down. She says." To the other question: "Behind. The Warm One is behind the door. Other side is only up."

Then he takes the broom in both claws, turns, and goes back down until he is about ten feet below Bram's boot. He puts the twigs to the stone and sweeps the same step over and over, *shhk, shhk*, waiting for you to follow.

Arren's wedge still leans face-up against the left pillar in the sun.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 5

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 5 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram falls in behind Ixx without taking his eyes off the little figure, shield low across his body and sword still in hand, not sheathed.

"Pell, light at his feet and the step ahead of me, nothing more," he says quietly. "Sefa, you're our eyes behind. If the air thickens or the walls stop being plain stone, say so." Then, aimed at the kobold's back: "Ixx. We're coming. You stop when I tell you, and we stop when the Warm One says stop. Ten feet, no more." He doesn't look back at the wedge of stone by the pillar, but his jaw is set. "And we'll leave that stone face-down before we leave here, no matter which way it goes."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa stays crouched a moment longer, then rises and goes back to the left pillar. She kneels and turns Arren's wedge over gently onto its face, pressing it back into the hollow it came from, into the ring of little prints. She smooths the turf around it with her palm.

"There," she says down to Ixx. "Asleep again. Your elders can have a quiet night." Then, softer: "I've read it. I won't forget what it said."

She dusts off her hands, comes back to the head of the stair and stops at Bram's shoulder. Her voice is low enough that it shouldn't carry ten feet down.

"Here's what I read in him. The Warm One's sealed in behind that door, and the door leads *out*. Arren's words are on it too. Ixx isn't afraid of his master, he's proud of him. What scares him is the door opening. His clan lives by serving whatever's down there, and if it gets loose they're nothing to it. Or worse." She glances at the lintel. "*Old gold, from before.* Big, warm, and talks fancy. I'd bet my beads it's a dragon, or something that thinks like one."

She meets Bram's eyes, then Pell's.

"We go down and we listen. We don't open anything, not for any amount of gold, until we know what Arren shut in and why. The stone says *let nothing of the fire go up*. I'm inclined to take a dead man's word over a live dragon's."

She takes her place behind them both, watching the walls and the stair at their backs as Bram ordered. Her hand rests on the Book of Shadows. Before she steps down she calls gently to the kobold:

"Ixx, sweep on. We're coming. And when we reach the bottom, tell me one more thing if you can: what does your clan do, if the Warm One gets angry with *us*?"

She follows Bram and Pell into the shadow of the stair, counting the steps under her breath, the way she used to count beds on the ward in the dark.

Pell Dunmore: “I’ll see to the stone, Sefa.”

I lay the wedge gently back in its hollow, lettering face-down, without covering it with earth.

Then I follow behind Bram’s shield arm, keeping the lantern low and watching the steps and walls as we descend.

Quietly to Bram and Sefa: “We’re agreeing to talk, not to open it. If the same warning’s on the door, give me time to read it before anyone touches the fittings.”

To Ixx, in an ordinary voice: “Thank you for telling us.”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram goes down behind the kobold with his shield low across his body and his sword bare in his hand. Ixx looks up at "ten feet, no more" and sets his broom to the next step down.

Sefa gets to the stone first. She kneels at the left pillar, rolls Arren's wedge onto its face and presses it back into its hollow, inside the ring of little prints. She smooths the turf round its edges with her palm. Below the sunlight, Ixx watches every bit of it with his head cocked. When she says *asleep again*, his tail lets go of his ankle all at once.

Pell said he'd see to the stone, but it's already down when he reaches it. He crouches, tips it a finger's width to check the letters are against the earth, and lets it settle. Then he stands up and takes his place behind Bram's shield arm, with the lantern hood dropped so the light lies on the step and no farther.

*(Out of game: Sefa's paragraph came first, so she turned the stone. Pell finds it done and only checks it.)*

When Pell thanks him, Ixx stops sweeping. He stands with the twigs on the stone and looks up at Pell as if he'd been handed something and doesn't know which pocket it goes in. Then he bows too low and too fast, and starts sweeping again.

---

The stair goes down a long way.

For the first thirty steps it's cut straight into the hill. The walls are rough grey rock, and roots hang through the cracks overhead. Then the rock stops. Sefa, watching the walls as she was asked, sees it change first: the raw stone gives way to dressed blocks, squared and fitted without mortar. Every so often one carries a mason's mark the size of a thumbprint, a little tower like the one on the lintel. The steps get wider and more even here. This part was built, not dug.

There are niches set into the wall every dozen steps, at shoulder height. Each is black with old soot, and in a few of them sits a stub of greasy kobold-made candle, cold. Goat droppings have been swept into the corners of the landings in tidy little heaps. Somebody comes back for those, too.

It gets warmer the whole way. By the fiftieth step Bram can feel the heat through the rings of his mail at the collar. By the eightieth the air is dry enough to catch in the throat, like the air in a bakehouse before dawn. It doesn't smell of smoke, only of hot stone and dust. Far below, under the sound of the broom, something too low to hear makes the stair hum very faintly underfoot, the way a floor does above a working mill.

Sefa's count reaches a hundred and twelve when the steps end.

The bottom is a landing of the same dressed stone, about twenty feet across, roofed with a low vault. Iron rings for lamps are set into the walls. They're rusted and empty. Two ways lead off it.

Straight ahead, a wide passage slopes on down. The warm breath comes up out of it, and so does the hum. Far down at its end, past where Pell's light can reach, there's a dull orange smudge on the dark, like the glow of a forge seen through a shutter.

To the left, a hole has been knocked through the wall at about the height of Bram's chest. It's rough-edged and kobold-sized, and it opens on a tunnel with a dirt floor. It smells of goat, tallow and a lot of bodies living close together. As your boots touch the landing, a noise comes out of it: many thin voices, high and quick, all talking over one another. Then one of them barks a word, and they go dead quiet. In that quiet you can feel them listening.

Ixx leans his broom against the wall. He turns to Sefa and answers the question she gave him at the top, quietly, the way he said the true thing before.

"Warm One angry, clan does what he says. Slings. Teeth. Clan is many." He glances at the hole in the wall, and his tail creeps back round his ankle. "Ixx asks you. If he is angry, not in there. Little ones in there."

Then he straightens his apron and gives you the next part of the message as if it had been there all along.

"Here Ixx goes ahead. Tells the Warm One: three freehands, ready to deal. Tells him he says first what is behind the door, and what of the last ones." He looks at Bram for that. "The tall ones wait here, on the landing. Or in the warren, with the elders, by the fire. Elders want to see who turned the stone." His eyes go to Sefa and stay there. "Ixx comes back. Not long."

He waits with one claw on the broom handle, looking from Bram to the sloping passage and back. He's ready to go when you let him, and he won't go before.
````

We're coming. And when we reach the bottom, tell me one more thing if you can: what does your clan do, if the Warm One gets angry with *us*?" As you descend you can hear her counting the steps under her breath.

````text
When you thank him, Ixx stops sweeping. He stands with the twigs on the stone and looks up at you as if he'd been handed something and doesn't know which pocket it goes in. Then he bows too low and too fast, and starts sweeping again.

---

The stair goes down a long way.

For the first thirty steps it's cut straight into the hill. The walls are rough grey rock, and roots hang through the cracks overhead. Then the rock stops. The raw stone gives way to dressed blocks, squared and fitted without mortar. Every so often one carries a mason's mark the size of a thumbprint, a little tower like the one on the lintel. The steps get wider and more even here. This part was built, not dug.

There are niches set into the wall every dozen steps, at shoulder height. Each is black with old soot, and in a few of them sits a stub of greasy kobold-made candle, cold. Goat droppings have been swept into the corners of the landings in tidy little heaps. Somebody comes back for those, too.

It gets warmer the whole way. By the eightieth step the air is dry enough to catch in the throat, like the air in a bakehouse before dawn. It doesn't smell of smoke, only of hot stone and dust. Far below, under the sound of the broom, something too low to hear makes the stair hum very faintly underfoot, the way a floor does above a working mill.

Then the steps end.

The bottom is a landing of the same dressed stone, about twenty feet across, roofed with a low vault. Iron rings for lamps are set into the walls. They're rusted and empty. Two ways lead off it.

Straight ahead, a wide passage slopes on down. The warm breath comes up out of it, and so does the hum. Far down at its end, past where your light can reach, there's a dull orange smudge on the dark, like the glow of a forge seen through a shutter.

To the left, a hole has been knocked through the wall at about the height of Bram's chest. It's rough-edged and kobold-sized, and it opens on a tunnel with a dirt floor. It smells of goat, tallow and a lot of bodies living close together. As your boots touch the landing, a noise comes out of it: many thin voices, high and quick, all talking over one another. Then one of them barks a word, and they go dead quiet. In that quiet you can feel them listening.

Ixx leans his broom against the wall. He turns to Sefa and answers the question she gave him at the top, quietly, the way he said the true thing before.

"Warm One angry, clan does what he says. Slings. Teeth. Clan is many." He glances at the hole in the wall, and his tail creeps back round his ankle. "Ixx asks you. If he is angry, not in there. Little ones in there."

Then he straightens his apron and gives you the next part of the message as if it had been there all along.

"Here Ixx goes ahead. Tells the Warm One: three freehands, ready to deal. Tells him he says first what is behind the door, and what of the last ones." He looks at Bram for that. "The tall ones wait here, on the landing. Or in the warren, with the elders, by the fire. Elders want to see who turned the stone." His eyes go to Sefa and stay there. "Ixx comes back. Not long."

He waits with one claw on the broom handle, looking from Bram to the sloping passage and back. He's ready to go when you let him, and he won't go before.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 6

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 6 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram keeps his sword low but not sheathed. He shifts his weight, plants his feet where the vault covers him, and glances once at the dark mouth of the warren before he answers Ixx.

"We'll wait here on the landing. Not in the warren. Old road habit: you don't set your back to a wall you haven't walked around yet." He says it plainly, like a rule of weather, not a threat. "Go and tell the Warm One we came down because you asked us to. Three freehands, ready to deal. Tell him we won't put a hand to the door until he tells us, straight, what's behind it and what happened to the last. If the elders want to see us, they can come out here where I can see the stair."

He turns his head slightly toward Pell and Sefa without taking his eyes off the two ways ahead. "Pell, hood the lantern down, light on the floor. Let them see we're not prying into every corner. Sefa, watch the warren's mouth and our backs. I'll watch the passage."

Then, to Ixx, a little softer: "Take your broom. You can leave it here if they'll fret over it, but it's your tool and I won't keep a man from his work. Tell him I said we leave the door shut unless he gives us good reason." He pauses. "Good reason means the truth, not the gold first."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa looks at the hole in the wall for a long moment, listening to that held breath of silence behind it. Then she nods to Ixx.

"Go on, Ixx. Tell him just as you said. We'll be here when you come back." She adds, gently: "And if he's angry, we won't bring it in there. Not near the little ones. You have my word on that too."

Once he's gone she turns to Bram and Pell, keeping her voice low.

"The elders want to see who turned the stone. I think I should go. They've lived beside this fire and those words their whole lives. They'll know what Arren's door says, and what the Warm One says about it when he's angry. I'd rather hear that before we stand in front of him than after." She glances down the sloping passage at the orange smudge. "Bram, you hold the landing. It's the only way back up, and if Ixx comes back or anything else comes up that slope, you'll see it first. Pell, will you come with me as far as the hole? Stand where you can see me and still shout to Bram. I'll keep it short."

She unclips nothing and draws nothing. She leaves the Book on her belt and her hands empty and in plain sight. At the knocked-through hole she stops on the landing side, crouches so her head is lower than the opening, and speaks in through it, soft and clear:

"Elders of Ixx's clan. I'm Sefa. I'm the one who turned Arren's stone, and I put it back to sleep with my own hands. Ixx asked me not to bring trouble among your little ones, and I won't. May I come in and sit by your fire a while? I'd like to ask what you know of Arren's words, and of the door. I'll listen more than I speak."

She waits there, still and patient. She won't go through until someone inside answers. All the while she keeps half an ear on the passage behind her, for the sound of a broom coming back up.

Pell Dunmore: “We’ll wait here, Ixx. I’ve no wish to bring a fight among your little ones.”

I move to the stair-side edge of the landing, leaving both passages clear and keeping the lantern shaded away from the warren.

Once Ixx has gone ahead, I speak quietly to the others. “If he orders the clan at us, this landing puts us between them and him. Let’s keep the stairs clear.”

I study the stone around the descending passage from where we stand—looking for more writing, fittings, or marks of how those before us met their end. I don’t touch anything or venture farther down.
````

**Card** · Pell Dunmore · Investigation: reading the passage mouth for signs of the last ones, from a distance, touching nothing · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Pell Dunmore · Investigation: reading the passage mouth for signs of the last ones, from a distance, touching nothing · decide · presses: [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram sets his feet under the lip of the vault, where the roof covers him and the stair is at his back. The warren's hole is on his left and the sloping passage runs straight ahead. His sword stays low and bare. Ixx listens to all of it with his head cocked, the way he listened at the top.

When Bram tells him the broom is his tool and nobody will keep him from his work, the kobold looks at the broom leaning against the wall, then up at Bram. He seems to have been told something about it he didn't know. He picks it up in both claws.

"Broom goes. Ixx sweeps in front of the Warm One. Always." Then he says the end of the message again under his breath, slowly, setting the words in order to carry. "Truth. Not gold first."

Sefa's promise about the little ones hits him harder than Bram's terms did. He looks at the hole in the wall, then at her, and gives two quick, low bobs of his head. When Pell says he wants no fight among the little ones, Ixx bobs again, a little smaller.

"Ixx tells. Not long."

He goes down the sloping passage, and he sweeps even with nobody behind him. *Shhk, shhk*, one stride at a time, the sound getting thinner. Bram watches the small shape go down against the orange smudge until he can't tell it from the dark. Then the sweeping is lost under the hum.

Pell takes the stair-side edge of the landing and turns the lantern's shutter so the light pools on the flagstones, away from the warren. From there he can see the hole, the passage and the stair, and Bram is a word away. He says his piece about keeping the stairs clear, low enough that it stays on the landing.

Sefa crouches by the hole on the landing side, her hands empty, and speaks in through it.

For a while nothing answers. The quiet inside is not empty, though. She can hear breathing, a great many small lungs, and something shifting on dirt. Then a light comes.

It's a candle stub in a cup of fired clay, held in a claw that is more grey than red. The kobold holding it is old, older than any of the clan Sefa has seen. Her scales have faded to the colour of cold ash, the little horns on her brow are worn down to nubs, and one eye is filmed milk-white. A string of goat knucklebones hangs round her neck and clicks when she moves. She stops just inside the hole, close enough that Sefa could touch her, and comes no further.

She studies Sefa's face for a moment. Then she looks at her hands for much longer, first one and then the other, as if she expected to find something on them.

"Kesh," she says. Her Common comes slower than Ixx's, with fewer gaps, worn smooth by long use. "Eldest. We heard you. All of it." The milky eye goes past Sefa to Bram under the vault. "Shield-man wants elders out where he sees. Kesh is old. Kesh does not walk out to iron."

She looks back to Sefa. "Arren's words. We do not read. The Warm One reads. The same scratches are on the door, under the little lamp. When he is angry he says them to the door. *Arren. Lent. Lent.*" She shapes the word like someone repeating a language she doesn't speak. "Then the little lamp gets bright, like sun on the top step, and he goes back from it. After that he is quiet a long time."

She turns the clay cup a quarter turn in her claw.

"Before the Warm One came down, moss grew to the third step. Kesh remembers. Ixx does not, Ixx was small. Now moss stops at the arch, and every winter the stair is warmer."

---

Across the landing, Pell goes over the mouth of the passage with his eyes, from the edge of the lantern light, without stepping off his edge or touching anything. He's looking for writing, fittings, and anything the last ones left behind.

Pell reads the passage mouth the way he'd read a doorway in a counting-house, and it gives up more than the stair did.

The arch is squared and built of the same dressed stone as the stair, with the little tower mark cut at the foot of each jamb. Both jambs carry iron pins as thick as a thumb, set in lead at the height of a man's knee and again above his head. A gate hung here once, heavy enough to need four hinges. The pins have been sheared off clean, and the stumps are rusted brown with age. Above the arch runs a band of letters cut deep and neat. They aren't Common, and they aren't anything Pell has seen painted on a Gate warehouse.

Two things are new.

At the right jamb, a hand's width off the floor, the grime has been scraped from the stone in a short pale gouge. Something heavy and iron-shod was set down against the wall there, or dragged past it.

In the corner, in a swept heap of goat droppings, something small catches the light. It's a hobnail with a square head, the kind a cobbler drives into the sole of a marching boot. Kobolds go barefoot. It's still bright grey, not rust-red, though in air this dry Pell can't say whether that means days or years.

---

Under the vault, Bram feels the breath change.

The slow, even warmth from the passage falters, and for the space of a heartbeat there is no draught at all. Then a gust comes up the slope, hard enough to stir the swept heaps and lift the hair on the backs of Bram's hands, far hotter than anything before it. It smells of hot iron and struck flint, with something rotten-sweet behind that, like bad coal. The hum under the floor stops while the gust lasts. At the far end of the passage the orange smudge flares, and for an instant it goes pale, nearly white. Then it sinks back to orange, the hum returns, and the breath settles into its old slow rhythm.

Behind Kesh the warren had been whispering again. Every voice stops. There's a soft sound of many small bodies pressing flat to the dirt.

Kesh doesn't go flat. She looks down the passage past Sefa's shoulder with her one good eye, and the knucklebones click once.

"Ixx is there. He has your words now."

She looks back at Sefa.

"The tall ones before had a chisel. Black iron, cold. They did not sit at our fire. They did not ask Kesh anything." She lets that lie for a moment. "Shield-man says *good reason*. *Truth, not gold first.* The Warm One is old, and big, and talks better than Kesh. He will give you truth, and he will give you good reason. Then you open?"

She waits in the hole with the candle, not inviting Sefa in and not sending her away, until she gets her answer.
````

## Round 7

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 7 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram steps away from the stair-mouth so the light from Pell's lantern doesn't edge into the passage, and he answers Kesh without raising his voice.

"No promises made out of the dark, Kesh. You'll know that about men like me." His eyes flick toward the orange glow down the slope, then back to her. "The Warm One can have truth and good reason—and he'll have them in front of me, not through Ixx's broom. But opening a door is a different thing from hearing a man's reason. I don't put my hand to a door until I know what it keeps and what gets out with us, and neither do my people."

He inclines his head toward the warren's mouth, just enough that Kesh can read it. "Your little ones stay clear of whatever comes next. We'll keep our word on that."

Then, to the others but still loud enough for Kesh to hear: "Pell, stand where you can see the slope and the stairs both. If Ixx comes back, we let him come. If anything else comes up, you tell me. Sefa, stay by Kesh a moment, but don't go in unless you're bidden—and not past the candlelight." He plants his shield and lets the tip of his longsword rest against the stone. "We're not the last ones."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa doesn't answer at once. She holds Kesh's good eye and lets the hot gust die away before she speaks, low and plain.

"No, Eldest. Not because he talks well. A voice that talks better than you is the one I trust least." She glances toward the passage, then back. "Arren wrote *kept, not killed*, and *let nothing of the fire go up*. I'd want a very good reason to make a dead man a liar. I haven't heard one yet, and I don't expect the Warm One to give me one he hasn't polished first."

She keeps her hands still on her knees, palms up, in the candlelight.

"You looked at my hands a long time. What did you think you'd find on them?" She waits a breath. "I'll tell you why I ask. The little lamp on the door, the one that shines like sun on the top step when he's angry... I carry a light like that. It was lent to me too."

She leans a little closer to the cup of fire.

"Tell me about the ones with the black chisel. Did they go at the door, or at the little lamp? And when they were burned, was it the Warm One who burned them, or the lamp?" Softer: "And the moss. Every winter warmer, every winter the moss further back. Is the little lamp still as bright as it was when you were young? Or does it take him longer, now, to go back from it?"

Without turning, she pitches her voice just loud enough for the landing.

"Bram, Pell. The last ones brought a cold iron chisel and didn't ask anyone anything. And the stair's been getting warmer every year, moss creeping back from the third step to the arch. Whatever Arren built to hold him, it may be wearing thin by itself."

Then she turns back to Kesh and waits, listening for the answer and for the broom.

Pell Dunmore: “No. Hearing him out isn’t a promise to open it.”

I look toward Kesh. “If the lamp makes him stand back, and the stairs grow hotter each winter, I’d want to know what opening that door would do to everyone living here.”

To Bram and Sefa, I point out the sheared hinges, pale gouge and hobnail without disturbing them. “Heavy gate here once. Gone a long time. Boot nail there, and a newer scrape low on the right. There’s writing above the arch, too—not a script I know.”

I move nearer Sefa, staying on the landing and keeping Bram in sight.

“Kesh, did your clan burn the last visitors after they died, or did they die by fire? And do you know what became of their chisel?”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram moves away from the passage mouth until the lantern light stays on the flagstones, then answers Kesh in the same low voice he used for the terms. She listens with her good eye on him. When he says *no promises made out of the dark*, her snout dips once. It could be agreement. It could be an old woman marking a thing she has heard before.

"Shield-man does not promise," she says. "Good. The chisel ones promised Ixx gold on the top step. They did not have any."

She lets his word about the little ones pass without an answer, but she doesn't go back from the hole either.

Then Sefa says *no*. She says it plainly and explains why, and Kesh goes very still. The warren behind her has started whispering again, and it stops when Kesh doesn't move.

"No," Kesh repeats. Then: "Kesh has asked that question to herself many winters."

When Sefa asks about her hands, the old kobold answers at once, as if she has been waiting for it.

"Black. Iron-black, in the cracks of the fingers. The chisel ones had it. They had worked cold iron a long time." Her milky eye goes over Sefa's open palms once more. "You have none."

Then Sefa tells her about the light: lent to her too, like the little lamp. The knucklebones at Kesh's throat knock together as she pulls back a hand's width. For a moment Sefa can't read her face at all. The candle shakes in its cup.

"Low," Kesh says. Her voice is almost too quiet to hear. "Say that low. Sound goes down this stone. He hears feet on the top step, Ixx told you." She looks past Sefa's shoulder into the sloping passage and stays looking for the length of a breath. Nothing comes up out of it but the slow warm air. When she speaks again it's still quiet, and it's an order: "Do not show it to him. Not first. Not for gold."

She answers the rest one question at a time, in order, the way a person counts out coin she has decided to spend.

"The lamp. They went at the lamp, not the door. Struck under it with the black iron. Ixx heard it ringing all the way up the slope. The Warm One spoke to them through the door while they struck. What he said, Kesh does not know."

"Burned, the man asks." She turns her head a little toward Pell, who has come nearer along the landing's edge. From where he stands he can still see the passage mouth and Bram under the vault. "Both. They died by fire, then we burned them. Proper, on our fire, so they do not walk. Three of them." She lets that sit. "The fire that killed them was not his. Not the lamp's. Down the slope there is a long hot room. Holes in the floor where the heat comes up, and little burning ones asleep in the walls. Iron rings near the holes and they wake. The chisel ones rang iron going back through it, and they ran with fire on them." Her claw tightens on the cup. "They did not sit. They did not ask. Kesh would have told them."

"The fourth one ran the other way, into the room of burnt paper, before the long room. With the chisel." A small dry sound in her throat. "We do not go in there. Kesh does not know if he came out. The chisel is where he is."

She comes to Sefa's last question and doesn't answer it right away.

"When Kesh was small, the little lamp was like noon. You could not look at it." She looks at the band of yellow lantern-light on the floor between them. "Now it is like the top step at evening. When he says Arren's words it still goes bright, and he still goes back. But slower. Every winter, slower."

That's all she says about it. She doesn't say what she wants. She has told them how much the lamp has dimmed, to their faces, and she leaves it lying there.

---

While Kesh talks, Bram and Sefa see what Pell points to without his touching any of it: the four sheared gate-pins rusted brown in their lead, the pale fresh gouge low on the right jamb, the bright hobnail in the swept heap. Above the arch runs the band of deep, neat letters he can't read.

*(Out of game: Sefa can read the band over the arch the moment she turns to look; she's facing Kesh this round.)*

---

Far down the slope, under the hum, the broom starts. *Shhk. Shhk.* It's thin with distance, but it's getting louder. It's coming back up.

Kesh hears it before anyone. She draws back into the hole until the candle lights only her face and the dirt wall behind her, and she speaks only to Sefa.

"Ixx comes with his answer. Before he is here, or after: come and sit at our fire, stone-turner. You. Not the iron." Her good eye goes to Bram, then to Pell, and back. "The elders are waiting. And there are things by our fire the man asked about. Better you see them sitting down."

She waits in the dark of the hole, the broom getting louder behind you.
````

## Round 8

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 8 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram gives Kesh a short nod, then tells Sefa without looking away from the passage: "If you go in, you go in with empty hands and you don't go past Kesh's light. Pell stays at the mouth where he can see you and still call back. One shout, and I come through that hole shield-first, warren or no warren."

He shifts his weight back toward the stair so he can watch both doors at once. The broom is close now. He raises his voice just enough to carry down the slope: "Ixx. Up. We're at the landing, same as before."

Then he says to Kesh, not unkindly: "You'll get your chat, Eldest, if she wants it. But first we hear what the Warm One sent."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa nods once to Kesh. She keeps her voice as low as the old kobold asked.

"I'll come, Eldest. But let me hear Ixx's answer first, here, with the shield-man. Then I'll sit at your fire, and I'll keep the lamp to myself until you've told me why I should." She touches two fingers to the prayer beads, then lays them flat on the dirt at the hole's edge, a small promise. "Thank you for the warning."

She rises and turns, and as she does she looks up at the band of letters over the arch Pell pointed out. The Lantern's gift should make them plain at a glance, and she reads them before she goes back to the others.

Then she goes to Bram's shoulder and speaks barely above a breath, for him and Pell only:

"Kesh says sound carries down this stone, so keep it quiet. The chisel company went at the *lamp* on the door, not the door itself. They were cutting out the thing that keeps him back. The lamp's been fading for years; it was like noon when she was small, now it's like evening. Three of them died in a hot room further down, with fire-things in the walls that wake when iron rings. The fourth ran into a 'room of burnt paper' with the chisel and never came out." A pause. "And she told me not to show the Warm One what I carry. Not first, not for gold. I mean to listen to her."

If the band over the arch said anything worth knowing, she tells them that too, in the same low voice.

"When Ixx has said his piece, I'm going to sit with the elders. She asked for me alone. I'll stay where you can shout to me." She glances at Bram. "If he wants us down there, nobody walks through that long room carrying bare iron near the holes until I've heard everything Kesh has to say about it."

Then she turns to face the slope beside them, hands empty and visible, and waits for the broom to come up into the light.

Pell Dunmore: I nod to Kesh. “Thank you. We’ll mind the iron.”

Quietly to Sefa: “Go if you think it wise. I’ll stay within calling distance. We can wait for you before going farther.”

I return to the stair-side edge, where I can watch the slope without blocking the warren. I check that my sheathed blade and tools aren’t hanging loose to strike the stone, then hold the lantern low for Ixx’s return.

To Bram, under my breath: “Before that hot room, we wrap whatever might clink. And I’d like a look in the burnt-paper room—there may still be a fourth man to account for.”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram's call goes down the slope low and level. The broom answers it. *Shhk, shhk*, coming faster now.

At the hole, Kesh watches Sefa touch her beads and then lay two fingers flat on the dirt at the edge of the warren. The old kobold looks at the fingers for a long moment. She puts one grey claw down beside them, not touching, and then draws it back into the dark with her candle.

"After," she says. "Kesh waits."

Sefa stands up and lifts her eyes to the band above the arch.

The letters are cut deep and square, in a hand much older and steadier than Arren's hurried line on the lintel. The Lantern's gift makes them plain at once:

*THE CINDER CELLAR OF ULCASTER. WARDENS AND THEIR BIDDEN ONLY. STRIKE NO IRON ON THE BREATHING FLOOR.*

Bram hears the name when Sefa passes it on at his shoulder, under her breath. It's the same name he knows from the waystones. The school that burned on its hill had a cellar, and this is it.

Pell goes back to the stair-side edge. He checks that his sheathed shortsword sits snug and his picks are rolled tight in their oiled cloth, then holds the lantern low with the shutter half down, so the light makes a pool on the slope.

Far down the passage, beyond the reach of the light, something listens to the stone.

Ixx comes up out of the dark into the pool of lantern light and stops ten feet from Bram's boot, the way he left. He's breathing fast through his snout. The fringe of his apron has curled and darkened, as if he stood close to an oven door, and the twigs of his broom smell scorched. He sets the broom down across the stone, then picks it up again and holds it crosswise.

He shakes himself once, hard, and looks at Bram. When he speaks it's the recited voice, word laid on word, and now and then something that isn't Ixx's comes through it, a turn of phrase too long for his mouth.

"The Warm One hears. Three freehands, ready to deal. Truth, not gold first." His tail tightens. "He laughs. Then he says.

"*Behind the door is the Warm One. Before the door is the stair, and the stair goes up to the sky. The Warm One was under the sky once. He wants it back. That is all the door is.*"

Ixx swallows and goes on.

"*The last ones. Four, with cold iron. They came to the door and the Warm One spoke to them, as he speaks now. They did not listen well. They rang iron in the long hall going back, and the house woke and burned them. The Warm One did not touch them. The house of Ulcaster bites iron. Ask the little ones, they carried what was left.*"

He gives the warren hole a quick look, then turns back.

"*The shield-man sets terms. Good. Terms are said to faces. Come down to the door, all three, and the Warm One gives the rest to faces. Gold is after. He does not say gold first.*" Ixx stops. His jaw works. "Then he says, for the long hall: *walk soft, keep iron quiet, step where the broom steps.* He says Ixx sweeps you through." His tail is wound twice round his ankle now. "He wants you whole. He says so."

That's the end of the message. Ixx's own voice comes back, smaller.

"He is pleased. Pleased is good." He doesn't sound sure of it.

Behind the hole the warren is silent. Kesh's candle has come back to the edge of the dark. Her good eye is on Ixx's scorched apron, not on the tall ones.

Ixx looks from Bram to Sefa to Pell, and his eyes go last to the hole in the wall.

"Now, or after the elders," he says. "Ixx waits. Ixx sweeps you down when you say."

 *(Out of game: none of these needed a roll. They're words and footing with nothing uncertain riding on them, so here's what the last turn skipped.)*

Before the broom came up into the light, Bram spoke to Sefa without taking his eyes off the passage. If she went into the warren, he told her, she went with empty hands and no farther than Kesh's candle. Pell would stay at the mouth where he could see her and still call back. If she shouted once, he would come through that hole shield-first, warren or no warren. Then he moved his weight back toward the foot of the stair, to a spot where the slope and the hole were both in front of him. From there he sent his voice down the passage: "Ixx. Up. We're at the landing, same as before." The broom came faster after that.

He spoke to Kesh last, without any edge: she'd have her talk if Sefa wanted it, but first they would hear what the Warm One had sent. Kesh took it with one dip of her snout and kept to the edge of the dark.

Sefa crouched at the hole and answered Kesh in the low voice the old kobold had asked for. She would come, but only after she'd heard Ixx's answer here with the shield-man. She would keep the lamp to herself until Kesh had told her why she should. Then she thanked the eldest for the warning, and that was when Kesh said "After. Kesh waits," and drew her candle back.
````
