Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 9

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 9 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram lets the message settle. Then he looks at Ixx and gives the kobold a single nod.

"Tell the Warm One this from me," he says. "We'll come down to his door. We'll walk soft, we'll keep our iron quiet, and we'll step where your broom steps." He taps his shield once with the flat of his thumb. "But we come when we've heard what the elders have to say. Good talk's like good road: better walked by daylight than in the dark, and we have a little of both yet."

He turns to Sefa and says, low but open: "Go to Kesh. Empty your hands of anything that might chime, and leave the book at your belt, not in your hand. I'll hold here. Pell, stand at the warren's mouth with the light low. If she calls, or if anything moves that shouldn't, I come through that hole. If I call, she comes out at once, no argument."

Then, to Kesh's candle at the dark: "Eldest. One question before she sits with you: what wakes the things in the long hall? Is it any iron at all, or only iron that rings? A man in mail walks heavy, and I'd rather know the difference before I set foot on that floor." He plants his shield once more, eyes already moving to the passage. "And if the Warm One grows tired of waiting while we talk, let him wait a few minutes more. He's been down here a long time. A little patience won't kill him."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa keeps her eyes on Ixx's scorched apron for a moment longer than on his face. Then she speaks to the shield-man and Pell, barely above a breath:

"Over the arch, in an old, steady hand: *The Cinder Cellar of Ulcaster. Wardens and their bidden only. Strike no iron on the breathing floor.*" A small pause. "Ulcaster. I've heard that name in Beregost, some old school of mages that burned. And mark this: he says four came, and the house burned all four. Kesh says three burned, and the fourth ran into the burnt-paper room with the chisel and never came out. Either he doesn't know where that man went, or he'd rather we didn't go looking." She lets that sit. "He wants us *whole*. A man who wants a door opened wants his hands whole too."

Then she turns to Ixx and crouches again, so she isn't looking down on him.

"Thank you, Ixx. You carried it well, every word." She nods at his apron and the scorched twigs. "You stood close to him to bring it. Are you burned anywhere? If you are, show me. I was a healer before I was anything else."

If he's hurt, she'll lay a hand near it and quietly offer the Lantern's warmth, a single die's worth, no more, and she'll keep it small and hidden in her palm.

"Tell the Warm One we heard him, and we'll come to the door and say our terms to his face. But not yet. Kesh has asked me to sit at the clan's fire first, and it's rude to keep an eldest waiting." She gives him a small smile. "Will you wait here with the shield-man and Pell? Sweep the landing if you like. They won't mind."

She rises, touches Bram's arm once in passing, *I'll be within a shout*, and goes back to the hole with her hands empty and open. At the edge of Kesh's candlelight she stops and bows her head slightly.

"Eldest. I've heard his answer. Now I'll hear yours."

She ducks through, low, and goes no farther than the candlelight reaches.

Pell Dunmore: “After the elders. No sense ignoring advice the last company didn’t stop to hear.”

I keep my voice quiet. “Ixx, your apron’s singed. Was that the usual heat down there, or did something happen while you gave our message?”

I glance toward Sefa, then settle near the warren’s mouth, leaving her room to pass and keeping the slope in view.

“And before we walk it, can you show us how to make our gear safe? I can bind loose metal, but Bram’s wearing a fair bit of it. Does iron have to strike the floor, or will mail ringing against itself wake the house?”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram's message goes down to Ixx in the same plain voice as the terms. The kobold takes it word by word, and his lips move as he lays each piece in order: *come down… walk soft… iron quiet… where the broom steps… after the elders.* At *good road* and *daylight* his head tips to one side, but he lets those go.

At *a little patience won't kill him*, he stops moving his lips.

Kesh answers Bram's question from the hole. Her candle stays where it is and she doesn't come out to the iron.

"Feet, no. Floor hears feet all day. Ixx's feet, goats' feet." Her good eye goes down the rings of his mail to his belt. "Ringing wakes them. Iron that knocks on iron, iron that knocks on stone. Far from the holes they sleep deep. Near the holes they sleep light, like old ones." She stops a moment. "Your rings talk to each other, shield-man. Every step, a little. Little talk near a hole, they turn over in their sleep. Big talk, they wake." Her eye drops lower, to his boots. "Chisel ones had iron in their feet too. Every step, on the stone."

Sefa passes the band over the arch to Bram and Pell under her breath, Ulcaster's name and the warning about the breathing floor. Then she tells them what doesn't fit: the Warm One counted four burned, and Kesh counted three. She keeps it low enough that it doesn't carry to the kobold ten feet down the slope.

Then she crouches in front of Ixx and asks about burns.

He looks at her as if she'd asked about something on someone else. Then he holds out his claws, backs up, the way you'd show a cook your hands. The scales across the knuckles have gone dull and powdery, like brick left too near a hearth, and they're warm when she lays her fingers close. The skin under them is whole. Nothing is blistered and nothing is weeping. His snout is the same. Whatever he stood close to, his scales took it.

"Scales are for hot," he says. Then, more quietly: "Nobody asks Ixx that."

He doesn't take his claws back right away.

When she asks him to wait on the landing, he looks at the hole, at Bram, and down the slope, and he shakes his head.

"Ixx cannot wait. Warm One counts. Ixx is gone short, he counts short. Ixx is gone long, he asks why." His tail creeps round his ankle. "Ixx goes, tells. When tall ones come, shield-man calls down. Ixx hears. Ixx comes up and sweeps."

Pell's question about the apron gets a longer answer than Ixx has given anyone so far.

"Warm One laughs. Laugh is hot." He plucks at the curled fringe. "Ixx stands close because he talks low. When he laughs, hot comes out round the door, by the edges." He looks up at Pell. "Before, the door keeps all the hot. Ixx stands there many winters, apron is fine. This winter, edges let some."

The question about gear makes him look down at himself, at the twigs and the sack-cloth and his bare claws. "Ixx has no iron." Then he turns to the hole.

Kesh barks one word over her shoulder into the dark. Something scuffles, small claws on dirt. A moment later a bundle comes shoving out through the hole at knee height, pushed by hands too small to see: greasy unwashed goat fleeces, half a dozen of them, rank enough to make Pell's eyes water, tied with lengths of dried gut.

"Goats are his," Kesh says. "Skins are ours."

Ixx crouches by the bundle and shows Pell what to do, and Bram too, since it's mostly for him. He takes a fleece and folds it wool-side in over the scabbard of Pell's sheathed shortsword, where the chape would knock against a thigh or a wall, then binds it round twice with gut and a knot that pulls tight. He holds up a second fleece toward Bram's mail and mimes wrapping it across a shoulder and round a hip. Then he pats the air low, over the place where a sword hangs. He doesn't touch the iron himself. He only shows where.

Then he gets up, takes his broom, and stops at the mouth of the slope.

"Ixx tells him the first words. Come down, walk soft, iron quiet, after the elders." His eyes go to Bram and flick away again. "Last words, Ixx does not carry."

He goes. *Shhk, shhk.* The sound thins down the passage and the hum swallows it.

---

Sefa bows her head at the edge of Kesh's candlelight and ducks through.

The hole runs only a few feet through the wall, and then the tunnel opens out. Beyond the little pool of the candle is a low hall dug out of the hill's earth. Timber props stand under a ceiling of roots, and the floor is trodden hard. In the middle a fire of dried dung and tallow burns low in a ring of stones and gives off more smoke than light. All round the walls are scooped niches heaped with goat fleece, and in the niches and along the walls there are kobolds. Sefa stops counting at forty. They're pressed flat and still, with their eyes catching the fire like coals. Smaller eyes look out between the legs of the bigger ones and from under the fleeces. One of the little ones gets free and runs a few steps toward the stranger before a grown claw hooks it back by the tail. It squeaks, and everything goes quiet again.

Kesh doesn't lead Sefa further in. She lowers herself onto the dirt just where her own light ends and sets the clay cup down beside her knee.

"You stop at Kesh's light. Shield-man said it, Kesh heard." She settles her knucklebones on her chest. "Kesh stops at iron. Each has a place. So the fire comes to you."

She barks again, softer this time. Two elders come out of the ring by the fire, a thin grey male with a sling coiled at his belt and a heavy female with a little one on her back. They bring two things between them and set them down in the candlelight in front of Sefa, then back away.

The first is a basket of woven goat hair. It's full of bones, burnt brittle and grey-white and broken small, the way bones come out of a pyre that was kept going a long time. Near the top three lower jaws are laid side by side, set out on purpose so anyone can count them.

The second is an iron helmet, a plain round cap with a nasal bar, the kind a hired blade buys secondhand in any town on the Way. The left side has slumped. The iron has run like tallow by a hearth and set again in hard drips along the rim, and above the ear there's a hole melted clean through, as wide as two fingers. The leather lining is gone. Inside, the crown has gone blue-black and rainbow-coloured in rings.

"Chisel ones," Kesh says. "Three. The man asked: did they die by fire, or did we burn them after. You see both." She turns her head toward the helmet but doesn't touch it. "The hat is from the long room. It was like that when we brought it out."

She doesn't say anything for a while. The fire pops. Out on the landing, Pell's lantern makes a faint yellow edge round the hole behind Sefa.

"You asked for Kesh's answer." The old kobold keeps her voice so low that the elders by the fire lean in. "He said four, and the house burned four. Kesh says three. Kesh has said it, and Kesh will not say it again louder."

Then she looks at Sefa's hands again, the way she did the first time.

"Kesh asks now. One thing." Her good eye comes up. "Lent light. Can it be lent on? To a lamp that is going out?"

---

On the landing, after the broom has faded, the breath coming up the slope changes. It doesn't gust the way it did before. It just comes up warmer, and it stays warmer: a long, slow exhale that keeps going for longer than any lungs should manage, until the swept heaps in the corners stir and the flame in Pell's lantern leans away from the passage. Then it settles back. The hum underneath comes back lower than it was.

 *(Out of game: all four happened in the scene above. Bram's message went down with Ixx, Kesh answered his question about iron, Sefa passed on the inscription, and she found Ixx's claws unburned. None of them needed a roll, because nothing about them was uncertain.)*
````

**Bram Halloway's screen (the relay):**

````text

"Over the arch, in an old, steady hand: *The Cinder Cellar of Ulcaster. Wardens and their bidden only. Strike no iron on the breathing floor.*" A small pause. "Ulcaster. I've heard that name in Beregost, some old school of mages that burned. And mark this: he says four came, and the house burned all four. Kesh says three burned, and the fourth ran into the burnt-paper room with the chisel and never came out. Either he doesn't know where that man went, or he'd rather we didn't go looking." She lets that sit. "He wants us *whole*. A man who wants a door opened wants his hands whole too."

Pell keeps his voice quiet too. "After the elders. No sense ignoring advice the last company didn't stop to hear." Then, to the kobold: "Ixx, your apron's singed. Was that the usual heat down there, or did something happen while you gave our message?" He glances toward Sefa and settles near the warren's mouth, leaving her room to pass and keeping the slope in view. "And before we walk it, can you show us how to make our gear safe? I can bind loose metal, but Bram's wearing a fair bit of it. Does iron have to strike the floor, or will mail ringing against itself wake the house?"

Sefa crouches in front of Ixx so she isn't looking down on him. "Thank you, Ixx. You carried it well, every word." She nods at his apron and the scorched twigs. "You stood close to him to bring it. Are you burned anywhere? If you are, show me. I was a healer before I was anything else."

---

Your message goes down to Ixx in the same plain voice as the terms. The kobold takes it word by word, and his lips move as he lays each piece in order: *come down… walk soft… iron quiet… where the broom steps… after the elders.* At *good road* and *daylight* his head tips to one side, but he lets those go.

At *a little patience won't kill him*, he stops moving his lips.

Kesh answers your question from the hole. Her candle stays where it is and she doesn't come out to the iron.

"Feet, no. Floor hears feet all day. Ixx's feet, goats' feet." Her good eye goes down the rings of your mail to your belt. "Ringing wakes them. Iron that knocks on iron, iron that knocks on stone. Far from the holes they sleep deep. Near the holes they sleep light, like old ones." She stops a moment. "Your rings talk to each other, shield-man. Every step, a little. Little talk near a hole, they turn over in their sleep. Big talk, they wake." Her eye drops lower, to your boots. "Chisel ones had iron in their feet too. Every step, on the stone."

When Sefa asks about burns, Ixx looks at her as if she'd asked about something on someone else. Then he holds out his claws, backs up, the way you'd show a cook your hands. The scales across the knuckles have gone dull and powdery, like brick left too near a hearth. Sefa lays her fingers close. The skin under the scales looks whole. Nothing is blistered and nothing is weeping. His snout is the same.

"Scales are for hot," he says. Then, more quietly: "Nobody asks Ixx that."

He doesn't take his claws back right away.

Sefa goes on: "Tell the Warm One we heard him, and we'll come to the door and say our terms to his face. But not yet. Kesh has asked me to sit at the clan's fire first, and it's rude to keep an eldest waiting." She gives him a small smile. "Will you wait here with the shield-man and Pell? Sweep the landing if you like. They won't mind."

He looks at the hole, at you, and down the slope, and he shakes his head.

"Ixx cannot wait. Warm One counts. Ixx is gone short, he counts short. Ixx is gone long, he asks why." His tail creeps round his ankle. "Ixx goes, tells. When tall ones come, shield-man calls down. Ixx hears. Ixx comes up and sweeps."

Pell's question about the apron gets a longer answer than Ixx has given anyone so far.

"Warm One laughs. Laugh is hot." He plucks at the curled fringe. "Ixx stands close because he talks low. When he laughs, hot comes out round the door, by the edges." He looks up at Pell. "Before, the door keeps all the hot. Ixx stands there many winters, apron is fine. This winter, edges let some."

The question about gear makes him look down at himself, at the twigs and the sack-cloth and his bare claws. "Ixx has no iron." Then he turns to the hole.

Kesh barks one word over her shoulder into the dark. Something scuffles, small claws on dirt. A moment later a bundle comes shoving out through the hole at knee height, pushed by hands too small to see: greasy unwashed goat fleeces, half a dozen of them, rank enough to make Pell's eyes water, tied with lengths of dried gut.

"Goats are his," Kesh says. "Skins are ours."

Ixx crouches by the bundle and shows Pell what to do, and you too, since it's mostly for you. He takes a fleece and folds it wool-side in over the scabbard of Pell's sheathed shortsword, where the chape would knock against a thigh or a wall, then binds it round twice with gut and a knot that pulls tight. He holds up a second fleece toward your mail and mimes wrapping it across a shoulder and round a hip. Then he pats the air low, over the place where your sword hangs. He doesn't touch the iron himself. He only shows where.

Then he gets up, takes his broom, and stops at the mouth of the slope.

"Ixx tells him the first words. Come down, walk soft, iron quiet, after the elders." His eyes go to you and flick away again. "Last words, Ixx does not carry."

He goes. *Shhk, shhk.* The sound thins down the passage and the hum swallows it.

Sefa rises, touches your arm once in passing, and goes back to the hole with her hands empty and open. At the edge of Kesh's candlelight she stops and bows her head slightly.

"Eldest. I've heard his answer. Now I'll hear yours."

She ducks through, low, and is gone past the candle. Whatever is said beyond the hole is too low to carry out to you. Pell's lantern makes a faint yellow edge round the opening.

---

After the broom has faded, the breath coming up the slope changes. It doesn't gust the way it did before. It just comes up warmer, and it stays warmer: a long, slow exhale that keeps going for longer than any lungs should manage, until the swept heaps in the corners stir and the flame in Pell's lantern leans away from the passage. Then it settles back. The hum underneath comes back lower than it was.
````

````text
"Tell the Warm One this from me," he says. "We'll come down to his door. We'll walk soft, we'll keep our iron quiet, and we'll step where your broom steps." He taps his shield once with the flat of his thumb. "But we come when we've heard what the elders have to say. Good talk's like good road: better walked by daylight than in the dark, and we have a little of both yet."

Then he gets up, takes his broom, and stops at the mouth of the slope.

---

You bow your head at the edge of Kesh's candlelight and duck through.

The hole runs only a few feet through the wall, and then the tunnel opens out. Beyond the little pool of the candle is a low hall dug out of the hill's earth. Timber props stand under a ceiling of roots, and the floor is trodden hard. In the middle a fire of dried dung and tallow burns low in a ring of stones and gives off more smoke than light. All round the walls are scooped niches heaped with goat fleece, and in the niches and along the walls there are kobolds. You stop counting at forty. They're pressed flat and still, with their eyes catching the fire like coals. Smaller eyes look out between the legs of the bigger ones and from under the fleeces. One of the little ones gets free and runs a few steps toward you before a grown claw hooks it back by the tail. It squeaks, and everything goes quiet again.

Kesh doesn't lead you further in. She lowers herself onto the dirt just where her own light ends and sets the clay cup down beside her knee.

"You stop at Kesh's light. Shield-man said it, Kesh heard." She settles her knucklebones on her chest. "Kesh stops at iron. Each has a place. So the fire comes to you."

She barks again, softer this time. Two elders come out of the ring by the fire, a thin grey male with a sling coiled at his belt and a heavy female with a little one on her back. They bring two things between them and set them down in the candlelight in front of you, then back away.

The first is a basket of woven goat hair. It's full of bones, burnt brittle and grey-white and broken small, the way bones come out of a pyre that was kept going a long time. Near the top three lower jaws are laid side by side, set out on purpose so anyone can count them.

The second is an iron helmet, a plain round cap with a nasal bar, the kind a hired blade buys secondhand in any town on the Way. The left side has slumped. The iron has run like tallow by a hearth and set again in hard drips along the rim, and above the ear there's a hole melted clean through, as wide as two fingers. The leather lining is gone. Inside, the crown has gone blue-black and rainbow-coloured in rings.

"Chisel ones," Kesh says. "Three. The man asked: did they die by fire, or did we burn them after. You see both." She turns her head toward the helmet but doesn't touch it. "The hat is from the long room. It was like that when we brought it out."

She doesn't say anything for a while. The fire pops. Behind you, Pell's lantern makes a faint yellow edge round the hole.

"You asked for Kesh's answer." The old kobold keeps her voice so low that the elders by the fire lean in. "He said four, and the house burned four. Kesh says three. Kesh has said it, and Kesh will not say it again louder."

Then she looks at your hands again, the way she did the first time.

"Kesh asks now. One thing." Her good eye comes up. "Lent light. Can it be lent on? To a lamp that is going out?"
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Pell Dunmore] [Sefa Marrin]

## Round 10

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 10 — one paragraph per character in this scene. Sefa Marrin is elsewhere this round, in a scene of their own that comes to you as its own message: do not narrate that character here.)

Bram Halloway: Bram watches Ixx go until the broom is lost under the hum, then lets his shield rest on the floor. He takes one of the fleeces and begins binding it round his scabbard with the gut, pulling the knots tight, then works another over the heads of the javelins.

"That long breath wasn't the house." He says it without looking up. "That was him, reminding us he's down there. Or tasting our talk." He tucks the last fold under his belt and drapes a fleece over his mail at the shoulders, leaving his sword arm free. "Muffle what you can, Pell. I'll keep the blade ready, but I don't want it knocking a stone in that long hall."

He straightens and takes up his place again, shield toward the slope. "When Sefa comes out, we compare Kesh's story and his. The last crew went at the lamp, not the door. If there's a fourth man still back in that burnt-paper room, he may have left more than a chisel. Before anyone puts a hand to that door, I'd like to know whether the floor's already had his iron." He keeps his eyes moving between the passage and the warren mouth. "And if Kesh says the lamp's fading, I want to see it before he makes his case."

Pell Dunmore: I watch the lantern flame settle, then murmur to Bram, “The door’s leaking more this winter. Whatever he offers, we may have a failing lock to deal with—not just a prisoner asking out.”

I set the lantern securely beside me, away from the fleece, and help Bram bind the wool over the places Ixx indicated, if he’s willing. I pad my remaining loose metal as well, keeping my sword’s grip clear.

“We’ll want a clear path back. No running blind past those holes.”

Then I stay by the warren’s mouth, listening for Sefa and watching the slope while we wait.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram rests the shield's rim on the flagstones and kneels by the bundle. The fleeces are stiff with old grease, and the smell comes off them in a wave once they're unrolled. He folds the first one wool-side in over his scabbard, from the chape to a hand below the hilt, the way Ixx showed Pell, and binds it twice round with gut. He pulls each knot until the gut bites. The second fleece goes over the heads of his four javelins in their quiver, bunched tight so the iron points can't knock against each other when he moves. He drapes the third across his shoulders like a carter's cloak in the rain and tucks it under his belt, leaving his sword arm free.

Pell sets the lantern on the bare stone by the stair, well away from the wool, and comes over without being asked. Bram lifts his elbow and Pell works the fourth fleece round his hip, where the mail skirt swings against the scabbard. He knots it off at the back, where Bram's hands can't reach. Then he takes the fifth for himself. He wraps it round his two daggers in their sheaths and pushes a fold of it down into the quiver, over the arrowheads, until a shake of his hip gets nothing out of it but a dull pat. His shortsword grip stays bare.

The difference is plain once they're done. Bram shifts his weight from one foot to the other. Before, the rings at his collar and hip gave off a small dry chink. Now they make a soft rustle, like a sack being set down. It isn't silent, but it's close. He also smells exactly like a goat.

Bram's reading of that long breath gets no answer from the passage. The warm air keeps coming up the slope at its old slow pace and the hum stays low. Nothing down there says whether he's right.

While they work, something moves at the hole in the wall. A small snout pushes out just above the bottom edge, red as a fresh-fired brick and no longer than Pell's thumb. Two bright eyes follow. The little kobold looks at the lantern, then at Bram in his fleeces, and sniffs hard and long. It looks pleased with what it smells. A grey claw comes out of the dark behind it, hooks it gently by the tail and draws it back in. From somewhere inside comes a single squeak of protest, cut short.

Pell takes his place by the mouth of the warren again, with the slope in view and the lantern pool at his feet. Bram stands under the vault with his shield toward the passage. Through the hole come the low murmur of the fire and, now and then, the click of something small on dirt. Ten minutes go by like that.

Sefa is still inside, at the edge of Kesh's light. Nothing else happens on the landing before she comes out or someone calls down the slope, unless you want it to.
````

The second fleece goes over the heads of his four javelins in their quiver, bunched tight so the iron points can't knock against each other when he moves. He drapes the third across his shoulders like a carter's cloak in the rain and tucks it under his belt, leaving his sword arm free.

````text
"Muffle what you can, Pell. I'll keep the blade ready, but I don't want it knocking a stone in that long hall."

You set the lantern on the bare stone by the stair, well away from the wool, and go over without being asked. Bram lifts his elbow and you work the fourth fleece round his hip, where the mail skirt swings against the scabbard. You knot it off at the back, where his hands can't reach. Then you take the fifth for yourself. You wrap it round your two daggers in their sheaths and push a fold of it down into the quiver, over the arrowheads, until a shake of your hip gets nothing out of it but a dull pat. Your shortsword grip stays bare.

The difference is plain once you're done. Bram shifts his weight from one foot to the other. Before, the rings at his collar and hip gave off a small dry chink. Now they make a soft rustle, like a sack being set down. It isn't silent, but it's close. He also smells exactly like a goat.

He straightens and takes up his place again, shield toward the slope. "When Sefa comes out, we compare Kesh's story and his. The last crew went at the lamp, not the door. If there's a fourth man still back in that burnt-paper room, he may have left more than a chisel. Before anyone puts a hand to that door, I'd like to know whether the floor's already had his iron." His eyes keep moving between the passage and the warren mouth. "And if Kesh says the lamp's fading, I want to see it before he makes his case."

Bram's reading of that long breath gets no answer from the passage. The warm air keeps coming up the slope at its old slow pace and the hum stays low. Nothing down there says whether he's right.

While you work, something moves at the hole in the wall. A small snout pushes out just above the bottom edge, red as a fresh-fired brick and no longer than your thumb. Two bright eyes follow. The little kobold looks at the lantern, then at Bram in his fleeces, and sniffs hard and long. It looks pleased with what it smells. A grey claw comes out of the dark behind it, hooks it gently by the tail and draws it back in. From somewhere inside comes a single squeak of protest, cut short.

You take your place by the mouth of the warren again, with the slope in view and the lantern pool at your feet. Bram stands under the vault with his shield toward the passage. Through the hole come the low murmur of the fire and, now and then, the click of something small on dirt. Ten minutes go by like that.

Sefa has not come out yet. Nothing else happens on the landing before she comes out or someone calls down the slope, unless you want it to.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Pell Dunmore]

### Scene: Sefa Marrin (order: Sefa)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 10 — one paragraph per character in this scene. Bram Halloway and Pell Dunmore are elsewhere this round, in a scene of their own that comes to you as its own message: do not narrate them here.)

Sefa Marrin: Sefa kneels at the edge of the candlelight. Before she answers she bows her head over the basket of bones, touches her prayer beads, and murmurs the short words the hospice said over the dead it couldn't save. She doesn't touch the jaws or the ruined helmet. Then she looks up at Kesh and keeps her voice as low as the old kobold's.

"I don't know, Eldest. That's the true answer, and I won't dress it up." She turns her hands over in the light, empty. "What I carry was lent to *me*, for the sick and the dying. I've never tried to pour it into a stone or a lamp. I don't know if it would take, or what it would cost, or who'd come asking for payment." A breath. "But the sign over your arch is a lantern, and the one I answer to I call the Lantern. That isn't nothing. Whoever lit that lamp on the door may have drawn from the same well I do."

She leans a little closer to the fire.

"So I won't promise. But I'll look. I'll go down, stand before the door, and listen to him, and I'll look at that little lamp with my own eyes. I'll read Arren's words under it. If there's a way to feed it, the words may say so. I won't show him what I carry. You told me not to, and I believe you." Softer: "If there's a way, I'll try. Not for gold. For the little ones, and for the man who wrote *kept, not killed*."

She glances once at the helmet, then back.

"Help me first, if you can. The room of burnt paper: is it Ulcaster's, from when this was a house of wardens? Do your people know what's in there, besides the fourth man? And the lamp on the door. Is it shaped like the one over your arch, with a ring handle and one drop of flame?" She pauses. "And one last thing, Eldest, because I'd rather ask it here than learn it down there. If the lamp burned bright again, like noon, and the Warm One stayed shut in for good, what happens to your clan? Would he still need a broom?"

She waits, hands open on her knees, and listens.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Sefa bows her head over the basket and says the short words. They're the plain hospice ones for the dead nobody could save, said low and quickly, the way they were said on the ward between one bed and the next. She keeps her hands off the jaws and the ruined helmet.

The warren hears it. Nobody speaks, but there's a stir along the wall niches, small heads turning toward each other. The heavy female with the little one on her back comes two steps out of the ring, stops, and puts one claw flat on the rim of the basket. She holds it there for as long as the words last, then goes back to the fire without looking at anyone.

Kesh watches all of it with her good eye. When Sefa says *I don't know, Eldest*, the old kobold's snout comes up a little, the way it did for Bram's *no promises*. She hears Sefa out to the end: *I'll look. Not for gold. For the little ones, and for the man who wrote kept, not killed.* She doesn't thank her. She takes it the way you'd take a coin you're going to bite later, carefully, without closing her claw on it yet.

Then she answers the questions in order.

"Burnt paper." She lifts her chin toward the slope beyond the wall. "Down the long way, before the hot room. A door on the left. The wood is black, but it stands. Inside are shelves, tall as two of you, and paper on them, all black. Touch it and it falls. When the Warm One was new here, a little one went in and came out grey to the elbows. Grey, not burnt. The paper was burnt long before us." She pauses. "That room is the wardens'. The Warm One does not like it. He cannot see in there and he cannot hear in there. So we do not go."

"The fourth one." Her voice drops lower. "He called out two nights from in there. Tall-folk words, the same ones over and over. Then he stopped." The flame in the clay cup stands straight. "The ones who carried the hat said the three ran with fire on them. The fourth ran with light. Something small in his hand, and light between the fingers."

She leaves that alone and moves on.

"The lamp on the door. Yes. Ring on top, one drop inside, same as the arch." She raises one grey claw and draws the shape in the air between them: the ring, the drop. Then she touches the tip of the claw to the bottom of the drop she has just drawn. "Here. Since the chisel ones, a bite out of it. Small. Like a chipped tooth."

She lowers the claw.

When she comes to the last question she doesn't answer right away. Behind her, the fire pops and settles.

"Yes," she says. "He would need us. Goats. News. Feet on the stair. While the door holds, he cannot do without us. We are his hands." The milky eye turns toward the dark at the back of the warren, where the little ones are thickest. "If the door opens, he has hands of his own again."

She lets it lie there. She hasn't said what she wants, and she doesn't say it now, but she doesn't take back what she has said either.

After a while she reaches across the edge of her own light, the place she said she wouldn't cross, and lays two claws flat on the dirt beside Sefa's knee. It's the same thing Sefa did at the hole.

"Stone-turner. When you have looked at the lamp, and read the scratches under it. Before you do anything with your hands." Her voice is barely more than the hiss of the candle. "Come up and tell Kesh what you saw. Will you?"

 *(Out of game: Sefa's hospice words over the dead happened in the scene above. Nothing about saying them was in doubt, so no roll was needed.)*

*(Out of game: going down to the door is something Sefa means to do later, not this round. She's still at Kesh's fire, and the turn stopped on Kesh's question: will Sefa come back up and tell her what she saw before she does anything with her hands?)*
````

**Sefa Marrin's screen (the relay):**


## Round 11

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 11 — one paragraph per character in this scene. Sefa Marrin is elsewhere this round, in a scene of their own that comes to you as its own message: do not narrate that character here.)

Bram Halloway: Bram stays where he is, shield toward the passage, and lets the warren's quiet talk settle behind him. He checks the bindings on his scabbard and spearpoints once more, then looks over the landing itself—the gate-pins Pell marked, the bright nail in the swept heap, the old band of Ulcaster script above the arch. He keeps his voice low, just enough for Pell.

"While we wait: if we do go down, Sefa's first in the order, I put my shield in front of her." His eyes stay on the dark. "The Warm One wants to talk to faces. Let him see an open hand before he sees steel. But the sword stays with me." He shifts the fleece at his shoulder. "One more thing—if we come back through that long room pressed, nobody runs. We walk at my pace, in our tracks, even if the walls wake. Panic and iron will kill us faster than anything down there."

Pell Dunmore: I give Bram a small nod. “Burnt-paper room first, if we can reach it safely. Agreed.”

I settle beside the lantern, leaving the hole clear, and wait for Sefa.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram stays under the vault with his shield toward the slope. He runs his thumb along the gut bindings one more time: the scabbard, the javelin heads, the fleece at his hip where Pell tied it. Every knot holds. When he shifts his weight his mail rustles under the wool and nothing rings.

Then he looks the landing over in the lantern-light, slowly, the way he'd walk a wagon line before a pass. The four sheared gate-pins sit rusted in their lead. The hobnail still lies bright in its heap of goat droppings in the corner. Above the arch, the deep square letters Sefa read out mean nothing to his eye. They're just cuts in stone, older and steadier than anything on the waystones. The rusted lamp rings in the walls hang empty. Someone swept here this morning, and probably yesterday morning too. He finds nothing he hadn't already seen.

He gives Pell the order of march quietly: Sefa first, an open hand for the Warm One to see, his shield in front of her, and the sword staying with him. If they come back through the long room under pressure, they walk at his pace, in their own tracks, and nobody runs. His voice doesn't go past the edge of the lantern pool.

Pell nods. "Burnt-paper room first, if we can reach it safely. Agreed." He sits down on the stair-side flagstones beside the lantern, with his back half to the wall so the slope and the warren hole are both in front of him. He leaves the hole clear and turns the shutter down a little more.

The hum goes on under the floor, low and even. Warm air comes up the slope at its slow old pace, and the lantern flame doesn't lean. From the warren you can hear the murmur of the dung fire and now and then small claws on dirt. Once a single little voice starts up high and quick, and a grown one hushes it. A few minutes pass like that.

Nothing comes up the passage and nobody comes out of the hole. Nothing else happens on the landing until Sefa comes out or someone calls down the slope, unless you want it to.
````

### Scene: Sefa Marrin (order: Sefa)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 11 — one paragraph per character in this scene. Bram Halloway and Pell Dunmore are elsewhere this round, in a scene of their own that comes to you as its own message: do not narrate them here.)

Sefa Marrin: Sefa looks down at the two grey claws on the dirt beside her knee. Then she lays her own fingers flat beside them, close but not touching, the way Kesh did at the hole.

"Yes, Eldest. I'll come up and tell you what I saw before I put a hand to anything. That's a promise, the same as the stone."

She stays a moment longer, thinking about what Kesh said about the light, then speaks very quietly.

"The fourth man ran with light between his fingers. And since the chisel ones, there's a bite out of the lamp's flame." She meets Kesh's good eye. "I think he carried a piece of it away. Into the one room the Warm One can't see or hear." A breath. "If that piece is still in there, with him, it may be the first thing worth bringing back to the door."

She bows her head to Kesh, then to the heavy female by the fire, and her eyes go once to the little ones under the fleeces.

"Thank you for the bones and the hat. Telling a stranger the truth costs something. I know that."

Then she ducks back out through the hole onto the landing, brushing the earth off her knees, and goes straight to Bram and Pell. She keeps her voice so low they'll have to lean in.

"Three jaws in a basket. Three burned, not four. The helmet's melted clean through. The long room did that, not the Warm One." She swallows. "The fourth man ran into the burnt-paper room, the wardens' room, with something small and *shining* in his hand. Kesh says since the chisel company the lamp on the door has a chip out of the bottom of its flame. And the Warm One can't see or hear inside that room. That's why he says four burned. He doesn't know."

She looks at each of them.

"And the rest of it: while the door holds, the clan are his hands. If it opens, he doesn't need them. Kesh wouldn't say what she wants, but she didn't have to."

She nods at the reeking fleeces. "We wrap our iron, all of it, the way Ixx showed. Bram, I'll help you with the mail. Then I want to go into that room before we stand in front of him. It's the one place on this road he can't listen to us, and the one thing he'd rather we didn't find may be lying in there with a dead man." A small, tight smile. "Ixx said he'll count. Let him. We'll call down for the broom once we're wrapped, and tell Ixx we stop at the wardens' door first. If that sends him running to his master, then we'll know something too."

She picks up a fleece, wrinkles her nose, and holds it out to Bram.

"Shoulder first. Hold still."
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Sefa lays her fingers flat on the dirt beside the two grey claws. She doesn't touch them, the way Kesh didn't touch hers at the hole. The old kobold looks down at the four fingers for a moment, and then the claws go back across the edge of the light, slowly, to her own side.

"Kesh heard. Like the stone."

Then Sefa says her guess aloud, very quietly: the light between the fourth man's fingers, the bite out of the lamp's flame, the one room the Warm One can't see or hear. Kesh goes completely still. Behind her the dung fire settles, and one of the little ones under the fleeces sneezes. Nobody hushes it, because every grown kobold along the walls is watching the eldest.

She stays silent a long time.

"Kesh does not know what he carried," she says at last. "Kesh knows the lamp had no bite before, and had one after." Her good eye stays on Sefa's face. "If it is in there, it is the wardens'. Their room. Their thing." She leans in a hand's width. "Back to the lamp, stone-turner. Not past it."

She doesn't say what lies past the lamp, and she doesn't need to point.

"And wrap your hands for the paper. Grey to the elbows, the little one was. Grey for a tenday after."

When Sefa bows her head to the heavy female by the fire, the elder dips her snout once in return. The little one on her back has been watching Sefa over its mother's shoulder the whole time. It lifts one tiny claw, uncertain, as if it has seen someone wave and wants to try it. Its mother doesn't stop it.

Sefa thanks Kesh for the bones and the hat. The eldest takes the thanks the way she took the promise, without a word. She lifts the clay cup so its light follows Sefa to the mouth of the hole.

---

Sefa ducks back through onto the landing and brushes the dirt off her knees. It's warmer out here than by the clan's fire, and drier, and the hum is under her boots again.

Bram is under the vault where she left him, but he doesn't look the same. A goat fleece hangs across his shoulders like a carter's rain-cloak. More are bound with gut round his scabbard, over the heads of his javelins and round his hip. Every knot is pulled tight. Pell sits by the lantern on the stair side with the shutter low, and a fleece is wound round his daggers too. The smell reaches her before she reaches them.

She goes to them and gives them all of it in a voice so low they have to lean in: three jaws, the helmet melted through, the long room and not the Warm One. The fourth man went into the wardens' room with something small and shining in his hand, and the lamp has had a chip at the bottom of its flame since then. The Warm One can't see or hear in that room, which is why he says four. The clan are his hands only while the door holds.

Her voice hardly carries past their bent heads. Far below, under the hum, nothing changes. The warm air comes up the slope at its old slow pace, and the lantern flame doesn't lean.

The slope stays quiet. Sefa lays out what she wants: wrap the iron, call for the broom, stop at the wardens' door first, and see whether that sends Ixx running.

One fleece is left in the bundle Ixx unrolled, lying by the stair, stiff and rank. Sefa picks it up, wrinkles her nose, and holds it out to Bram.

"Shoulder first. Hold still."

Across his shoulders the first fleece is already tied off and tucked under his belt.

*(Out of game: Bram and Pell wrapped their gear while Sefa was inside, so his shoulders, scabbard, javelins and hip are done. This last fleece is the only one left, and Sefa's own dagger is still bare.)*
````

## Round 12

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 12 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram waits until the hush from the warren feels wrong, then crosses to the hole and leans his shield against the wall beside it, not blocking the entry. He keeps his sword hand free and his voice pitched low, no farther than the dark inside.

"Sefa. It's been a while. All's quiet out here." A pause. "Are you well, or do I come in?"

Sefa Marrin: Sefa looks at Bram's shoulders, already tied off and tucked, and lowers the fleece with a short, rueful breath.

"You were quicker than me. Good." She keeps her voice down. "Then this one's mine."

She kneels by the lantern and works fast. She draws her dagger and folds the fleece's thickest corner wool-side in over the blade and crossguard, then binds it twice with a length of gut the way Ixx showed, pulling the knot tight before she slides it back into its sheath. She tugs at it once to make sure nothing knocks. Then she runs her palm down her studded leather, feeling for rivets that might click against each other or a wall, and tucks what's left of the fleece under her belt over the worst of them.

The rest she tears into two rough strips with her hands and teeth and holds them up for the others to see.

"For the paper room. Kesh says a little one went in and came out grey to the elbows, grey for a tenday. Wrap your hands before you touch anything in there. Don't breathe it in, either." She hands one strip to Pell. "You'll be touching more than I will."

She stands, checks that the Book sits quiet at her belt, and looks at both of them.

"Order going down: Ixx sweeps, Bram behind him, Pell with the light low, me at the back. We step where the broom steps. Nobody stops near a hole in the floor. At the wardens' door, the black one on the left, we go in, and Ixx can wait outside or come, whichever he likes. If he balks, we let him tell his master. I'd rather the Warm One knew we'd gone in than have Ixx lie to him for us and pay for it." A pause. "Ready?"

When they are, she steps to the head of the slope and calls down, soft and clear, pitched to carry on the stone but no louder than it has to be:

"Ixx. We've sat with the elders, and we're wrapped. Come up and sweep for us, please."

Then she waits at the head of the passage with her hands empty and her eyes on that dull orange smudge far below, listening for the broom.

Pell Dunmore: “Slow feet, quiet iron,” I murmur. “Understood.”

I wait for Sefa to return.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Sefa lowers the fleece and lets out a short breath.

"You were quicker than me. Good. Then this one's mine."

She kneels by Pell's lantern and gets to work. She folds the thickest corner of the fleece wool-side in over the blade and crossguard of her hospice knife, binds it twice round with gut the way Ixx showed, and pulls the knot until it bites. The dagger goes back into its sheath, and when she tugs at it nothing knocks. She runs her palm down the front of her studded leather, feeling for studs that could click against each other or catch on a wall, and tucks a fold of the fleece under her belt over the worst of them.

The rest of the fleece doesn't want to tear. The hide is old and stiff with grease, and she has to set her teeth in it and pull with both hands before it gives along the edge with a dull rip. She spits twice. It tastes exactly the way it smells. In the end she has two ragged strips, and she holds one out to Pell.

"For the paper room. Kesh says a little one went in and came out grey to the elbows, and stayed grey for a tenday. Wrap your hands before you touch anything in there, and don't breathe it in. You'll be touching more than I will."

She checks that the Book sits quiet at her belt and gives them the order of march in a low voice. Ixx sweeps in front, then Bram, then Pell with the light low, and she goes last. They step where the broom steps and nobody stops near a hole in the floor. At the black door on the left they go in, and Ixx can wait or come as he likes. "If he balks, we let him tell his master. I'd rather the Warm One knew we'd gone in than have Ixx lie for us and pay for it."

"Slow feet, quiet iron," Pell murmurs. "Understood."

*(Out of game: Sefa came out of the warren at the end of last round, so Bram and Pell already have her on the landing with them. There's nobody inside to call for or wait on. I've taken Pell's "understood" as his answer to her "Ready?", and Bram still has the moment before Ixx arrives to stop or change anything.)*

Sefa steps to the head of the slope with her hands empty and pitches her voice to carry on the stone and no further.

"Ixx. We've sat with the elders, and we're wrapped. Come up and sweep for us, please."

At the hole behind her, the candle at the edge of the dark draws back a hand's width and stays there.

The answer comes sooner than any of them expect. *Shhk, shhk.* The broom is already well up the passage, as if Ixx had been waiting partway down rather than at the bottom. It climbs fast, faster than he has swept so far.

He comes up into the pool of lantern light and stops ten feet below the landing. He holds the broom crosswise and stays there with his eyes on the fleeces. He takes in Bram's shoulders and hip, the wrapped javelins, Pell's daggers and Sefa's knife, and his nostrils work. For a moment he looks very nearly proud of them.

Then the recited voice comes, word laid on word.

"The Warm One counts. He says: *elders talk long.* He says: *the Warm One is patient.*" Ixx swallows. "He says *patient* two times."

His tail has wound itself round his ankle. He waits a breath, but there's no more message, and when he speaks again it's in his own voice.

"Ixx sweeps. Down now?"
````

## Round 13

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 13 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram takes up his shield and steps to the head of the slope, just behind the edge of Sefa's lantern-light. He rolls his shoulders once under the fleece, hears only wool, and gives Ixx a single nod.

"Down now. Ixx, you sweep ahead of us—same as before, ten feet, no more. You stop where the broom stops. We don't pass you."

He glances back once at Pell and Sefa. "Order as she set it. I'm behind the broom. Pell, light low and on the floor, not on the walls. Sefa, last, and keep two steps between you and Pell. If I say *hold*, we all hold. If I say *back*, we turn on the spot we're standing and walk out at my pace. No one runs."

Then to Ixx, quieter: "Tell him we're on our way. And Ixx—if the brood's watching, keep them off the foot of the stairs behind us. We don't want little feet near that long room." He sets his shield toward the dark. "Lead on."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa takes the second strip of fleece from her belt and presses it into Pell's free hand without a word. Then she turns to the kobold and crouches at the lip of the slope, so she isn't standing over him.

"Down now, Ixx. Thank you for coming so quickly." She glances at his tail wound round his ankle, and her voice stays gentle. "Two times patient. We heard."

She lets a breath go before the next part, and says it plainly so he won't have to guess at it.

"There's a black door on the left, before the long hot room. The wardens' room. We're going to stop there first and look inside. Only look, and we'll be careful with our hands." She holds his eyes. "You don't have to come in. You can wait at the door, or go on and tell the Warm One where we are. If he asks, tell him the truth: the stone-turner wanted to see the wardens' room before the wardens' door. That's mine to answer for, not yours."

She rises, settles into the back of the line behind Pell's low lantern, and looks once over her shoulder at the hole in the wall, where the candle still waits a hand's width back in the dark.

"Sweep on, Ixx. We'll step where you step."

As they start down she keeps her eyes moving over the walls and the floor ahead, counting under her breath again, and watching for the first holes in the stone where the heat comes up. If she sees one, she'll touch Pell's shoulder and point before anyone's boot comes near it.

Pell Dunmore: I take the strip from Sefa. “Thanks. What else did Kesh tell you we need to know before we reach that room?”

I tuck it within reach, keeping my hands free for the lantern.

“Down now, Ixx. We want to look in the burnt-paper room on the way. You needn’t go inside, and we won’t ask you to hide it from the Warm One.”

I fall in behind Bram, keeping the light low and placing my feet where Ixx sweeps.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram steps up to the head of the slope with his shield on his arm. He rolls his shoulders, and all that comes from under the fleece is the soft rub of wool on wool. He gives Ixx one nod and sets out the order: Ixx sweeps ten feet ahead, nobody passes him, *hold* means everyone holds, *back* means everyone turns where they stand and walks out at Bram's pace.

Ixx takes the order the way he takes a message, mouthing the words as he hears them. When Bram asks him to keep the brood off the foot of the stair, he answers without stopping to think.

"Little ones do not go down the slope. Never. Kesh keeps them." He looks at the hole in the wall. "Kesh hears you anyway."

At the edge of the hole the candle goes out of sight.

Then Sefa crouches at the lip of the slope and tells him plainly about the black door on the left. They mean to stop there first and only look. If the Warm One asks, he should tell him the truth: the stone-turner wanted to see the wardens' room before the wardens' door, and that is hers to answer for. Ixx goes completely still.

His claws close on the broom handle. Air leaks out between his teeth with a short, wet hiss.

"Wardens' room." He says it under his breath, the way Kesh said *Arren*. "He cannot see in there. Cannot hear in there." His eyes go down the passage toward the orange smudge and back to her. "Ixx does not go in."

The next part takes him longer.

"Ixx tells true. Stone-turner wants to see. Stone-turner answers." He tries the words over once more under his breath, and whatever he finds in them seems to settle him. His tail stays wound round his ankle, but the rest of him stops looking like it's braced to run. "Ixx does not have to make words up. Good."

When Pell says the same thing in his own plain way, that they won't ask him to hide it, Ixx gives him a small bob of the head and turns to the slope.

*(Out of game: Pell's question about what else Kesh told her is Sefa's to answer, so I've left it for her player.)*

---

*Shhk, shhk.* They go down.

The passage is ten feet wide and built like the lower stair, dressed blocks fitted without mortar, with the little tower cut low on a stone every so often. The slope is gentle, but it never levels off. Down the middle of the floor runs a shallow groove, worn by a lot of feet over a lot of years, and that is where Ixx sweeps. Bram keeps ten feet behind him. Pell comes next with the lantern shutter down, and the light makes a yellow pool round their boots that goes no farther than the stone just ahead. Sefa walks two steps behind Pell, counting under her breath.

The heat climbs as they go. By her fortieth pace the air is like a bakehouse with the oven doors open, and the fleece on Bram's shoulders has started to sweat out its grease. The hum gets louder too. It isn't a sound you hear so much as one you feel coming up through the soles of your boots. The walls have shoulder-height niches like the stair, but here no kobold has left a candle in any of them.

Sefa watches the floor the whole way. Here it's solid stone and nothing more, swept clean and not even warm enough to notice through her boots.

At about a hundred and fifty feet down, the left-hand wall changes.

There's a door set into it under a squared arch. It's broad and made of oak, and the oak is charred black all over, crazed into little squares like a log pulled out of a fire. It still stands, and it's shut. The hinges, three of them, are dull green with age. They're bronze, not iron. Over the arch is a single carving, a lantern with a ring handle and one drop of flame, and no tower above it this time.

At hand height on the door's edge there's a smear. It's a palm-print, darker than the char around it, with four fingers dragged down from it as if someone pushed the door shut behind him while he was going down. The door doesn't sit flush. There's a gap the width of a finger between its edge and the jamb.

Air comes out of that gap, and it's cool. In all the cellar it's the first air that hasn't been warm. It brushes Pell's knuckles round the lantern handle and smells of old paper and nothing else. When Bram stands square in front of the door, the hum under his boots seems to fade, the way a mill goes quiet when you step in behind a thick wall.

Beyond the door the passage keeps sloping down. At the far edge of the light, forty feet or so on, the orange starts to show on the walls. Sefa can just make out the floor there: dark round openings in the stone, two and then a third, each with a faint shimmer of heat over it. They're well past where anyone means to set a foot tonight.

Ixx stops ten feet short of the door, the way he was told. He won't look at the lantern carved over the arch.

"Here. Black door." He shifts the broom from one claw to the other. "Ixx goes. Tells. Stone-turner looks at wardens' room." He hesitates. "When tall ones come out, call down. Ixx comes, sweeps the long room. Ixx knows where the holes sleep light."

He turns and goes down toward the orange, sweeping the whole way. The sound of the broom thins and blurs, and the hum takes it.

The three of you are standing at the black door with the lantern pool at your feet and the cool air coming out of the gap. Nobody has touched the door.

 *(Out of game: none of the four needed a roll, because plain words and handing something over carry no uncertainty. Three of them are already in the scene above, and the fourth is filled in below.)*

1) Bram gave Ixx the order at the head of the slope: sweep ten feet ahead, nobody passes him, *hold* means everyone holds, *back* means everyone turns where they stand and walks out at Bram's pace. Ixx mouthed the words as he heard them and kept to it all the way down to the black door.

2) Ixx answered Bram's ask about the brood at once: "Little ones do not go down the slope. Never. Kesh keeps them." The candle at the warren hole then went out of sight.

3) Ixx took Sefa's account of the black door and her words for the Warm One. Then he worked through it under his breath: "Ixx tells true. Stone-turner wants to see. Stone-turner answers." He has gone down to say exactly that.

4) Before they set off, Sefa pressed the second fleece strip into Pell's free hand without a word, and he took it. He tucked it into his belt where he could reach it, which left his hands free for the lantern.
````

````text
At the edge of the hole the candle goes out of sight.

When you tell him about the black door, Ixx goes completely still. His claws close on the broom handle, and air leaks out between his teeth in a short, wet hiss.

"Wardens' room." He says it under his breath, the way Kesh said *Arren*. "He cannot see in there. Cannot hear in there." His eyes go down the passage toward the orange smudge and back to you. "Ixx does not go in."

The next part takes him longer. "Ixx tells true. Stone-turner wants to see. Stone-turner answers." He tries the words over once more under his breath, and that seems to settle him. His tail stays wound round his ankle, but the rest of him no longer looks braced to run. "Ixx does not have to make words up. Good."

When Pell says the same thing his own way, Ixx gives him a small bob of the head and turns to the slope.

*Shhk, shhk.* You go down.

The passage is ten feet wide and built like the lower stair: dressed blocks fitted without mortar, with the little tower cut low on a stone every so often. The slope is gentle, but it never levels off. A shallow groove runs down the middle of the floor, worn by a lot of feet over a lot of years, and that is where Ixx sweeps. Bram keeps ten feet behind him. Pell comes next with the lantern shutter down, and the light makes a yellow pool round your boots that reaches no farther than the stone just ahead. You walk two steps behind Pell, counting under your breath.

The heat climbs as you go. By your fortieth pace the air is like a bakehouse with the oven doors open. The hum gets louder too. You don't hear it so much as feel it coming up through the soles of your boots. The walls have shoulder-height niches like the stair, but no kobold has left a candle in any of them here.

You watch the floor the whole way. Here it's solid stone and nothing more, swept clean and not even warm enough to notice through your boots.

About a hundred and fifty feet down, the left-hand wall changes.

A door is set into it under a squared arch. It's broad and made of oak, and the oak is charred black all over, crazed into little squares like a log pulled out of a fire. It still stands, and it's shut. Its three hinges are dull green with age: bronze, not iron. Over the arch is a single carving, a lantern with a ring handle and one drop of flame, and no tower above it this time.

At hand height on the door's edge there's a smear. It's a palm-print, darker than the char around it, with four fingers dragged down from it, as if someone pushed the door shut behind him while he was going down. The door doesn't sit flush. There's a gap the width of a finger between its edge and the jamb, and the air coming out of it is cool, the first air in the whole cellar that hasn't been warm.

Beyond the door the passage keeps sloping down. At the far edge of the light, forty feet or so on, the orange starts to show on the walls. You can just make out the floor there: dark round openings in the stone, two and then a third, each with a faint shimmer of heat over it. They're well past where anyone means to set a foot tonight.

Ixx stops ten feet short of the door, as he was told. He won't look at the lantern carved over the arch.

"Here. Black door." He shifts the broom from one claw to the other. "Ixx goes. Tells. Stone-turner looks at wardens' room." He hesitates. "When tall ones come out, call down. Ixx comes, sweeps the long room. Ixx knows where the holes sleep light."

He turns and goes down toward the orange, sweeping the whole way. The sound of the broom thins and blurs, and the hum swallows it.

The three of you stand at the black door, the lantern pool at your feet and cool air coming out of the gap. Nobody has touched the door.
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Bram takes up his shield and steps to the head of the slope, just behind the edge of Sefa's lantern-light. He rolls his shoulders once, and all that comes from under the fleece is the soft rub of wool on wool. He gives Ixx a single nod.

"Down now. Ixx, you sweep ahead of us—same as before, ten feet, no more. You stop where the broom stops. We don't pass you."

He glances back once at you and Sefa. "Order as she set it. I'm behind the broom. Pell, light low and on the floor, not on the walls. Sefa, last, and keep two steps between you and Pell. If I say *hold*, we all hold. If I say *back*, we turn on the spot we're standing and walk out at my pace. No one runs."

Then, quieter, to Ixx: "Tell him we're on our way. And Ixx—if the brood's watching, keep them off the foot of the stairs behind us. We don't want little feet near that long room." He sets his shield toward the dark. "Lead on."

Sefa takes a second strip of fleece from her belt and presses it into your free hand without a word. Then she crouches at the lip of the slope, low beside Ixx so she isn't standing over him.

"Down now, Ixx. Thank you for coming so quickly." Her eyes go to his tail, wound round his ankle, and her voice stays gentle. "Two times patient. We heard."

She lets out a breath before she goes on, and she says it plainly. "There's a black door on the left, before the long hot room. The wardens' room. We're going to stop there first and look inside. Only look, and we'll be careful with our hands." She holds his eyes. "You don't have to come in. You can wait at the door, or go on and tell the Warm One where we are. If he asks, tell him the truth: the stone-turner wanted to see the wardens' room before the wardens' door. That's mine to answer for, not yours."

She rises, takes her place at the back of the line behind you, and looks once over her shoulder at the hole in the wall. "Sweep on, Ixx. We'll step where you step."

---

Ixx takes Bram's order the way he takes a message, mouthing the words as he hears them. When Bram asks him to keep the brood off the foot of the stair, he answers without stopping to think.

"Little ones do not go down the slope. Never. Kesh keeps them." He looks at the hole in the wall. "Kesh hears you anyway."

At the edge of the hole the candle goes out of sight.

When Sefa tells him about the black door, Ixx goes completely still. His claws close on the broom handle, and air leaks out between his teeth in a short, wet hiss.

Sefa walks two steps behind you, counting under her breath.

The heat climbs as you go. Before long the air is like a bakehouse with the oven doors open. The hum gets louder too. You don't hear it so much as feel it coming up through the soles of your boots. It still stands, and it's shut. Its three hinges are dull green with age: bronze, not iron. Over the arch is a single carving, a lantern with a ring handle and one drop of flame, and no tower above it this time.

At hand height on the door's edge there's a smear. It's a palm-print, darker than the char around it, with four fingers dragged down from it, as if someone pushed the door shut behind him while he was going down. The door doesn't sit flush. There's a gap the width of a finger between its edge and the jamb.

Air comes out of that gap, and it's cool, the first air in the whole cellar that hasn't been warm. It brushes your knuckles round the lantern handle and smells of old paper and nothing else.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 14

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 14 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram stops at the edge of the cool air and lets it wash the wool and sweat off his face before he moves.

"Hold." He doesn't take his eyes off the door. "Pell, look at the edge and the hinges before we put hands on it. The print says someone shut it from the inside as he went down—or was dragged down and threw his hand back for purchase. I want to know if it's wedged, barred, or watched."

He angles his shield across his body and sets himself between the door and the slope. "Sefa, keep the light off the walls and watch the passage. If that orange flutters or Ixx stops sweeping, call it." He pauses. "And if this is the room Kesh won't walk into, we don't leave anyone alone inside it. Two in, one at the door always. The one at the door stands where I stand now, facing out."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa winds the strip of fleece round her right hand, wool inward, and ties it off with her teeth. She doesn't reach for the door yet. She stands looking at the dragged palm-print for a moment, then answers Pell, low.

"What else Kesh told me: the paper in there falls to grey at a touch. It's old burning, from before the Warm One. The fourth man called out for two nights from inside, the same tall-folk words over and over, and then stopped." She nods at the print. "That's likely him, shutting the door behind himself. And she said one more thing: *back to the lamp, stone-turner, not past it.* If we find what he carried, it goes back where it came from. Nowhere else."

She glances down the slope where Ixx went, then at the carving over the arch. It's a lantern with no tower this time.

"Bronze hinges. No iron in this door at all. Whoever built this room didn't want it to ring." She breathes in the cool air from the gap. "And it's cold in there. The only cold place in the whole cellar."

She steps back half a pace to give Pell the door.

"Pell, it's your trade. Look it over before anyone pushes, top to bottom. If it's clean, open it slowly, a hand's width at a time. Bram, watch the slope, not the room."

While Pell works, she bends close to the finger-wide gap, careful not to touch the char, and listens. Then she speaks softly into the dark beyond, the way she'd speak at the curtain of a sick bed before going in:

"We're coming in. We mean no harm to whoever's within, living or dead. We've come for what was taken from the door, and to put it back."

When the door opens, she won't step in first. She'll lift her wrapped hand and quietly cast Light on her own glove, a small steady glow that doesn't need the lantern. Then she'll look over the shelves, the floor, and whatever lies near the threshold before she sets a foot inside.

Pell Dunmore: I raise the lantern just enough to see the door’s edge and crouch without touching the palm-print.

“Cool air, bronze hinges. Let me check it before we push.”

I examine the gap, jamb, threshold and any latch for wires, catches or signs of a trap, keeping my face away from the opening. I also look for loose grey residue like Kesh described.

If it looks safe to handle, I wrap my hands in the fleece before trying to ease the door open, slowly, without scraping it against the floor.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
"Hold."

Bram stops at the edge of the cool air and lets it run over his face. After the slope it's like stepping out of a smithy into a cellar. He gives his orders without taking his eyes off the door. Then he turns, sets his shield across his body and plants himself between the black door and the slope, facing out, toward the orange glow at the far edge of the light. *Two in, one at the door always, facing out.* He says it once and leaves it there.

Behind him the hum is going. With the gap at his back it sounds like a mill heard through a thick wall, there and not there.

Sefa winds the fleece strip round her right hand, wool side in, and pulls the knot tight with her teeth. While she does it she answers Pell's question from the slope, low. The paper is old burning, from before the Warm One, and goes to grey at a touch. The fourth man called out from inside for two nights, the same tall-folk words over and over, and then stopped. And Kesh's last word was *back to the lamp, not past it*: whatever he carried goes back where it came from, and nowhere else. She looks at the bronze hinges, then at the lantern carved over the arch with no tower above it, and steps back half a pace to give Pell the door.

She bends to the finger-wide gap, keeping her face off the char, and listens.

There's nothing to hear. It isn't the held-breath quiet of the warren. It's the quiet of a shut room where nothing has moved for a long while. Cool air comes out past her cheek and smells of old paper.

"We're coming in," she says into the gap, the way you'd speak at the curtain round a sick bed. "We mean no harm to whoever's within, living or dead. We've come for what was taken from the door, and to put it back."

Nothing answers her.

---

Pell crouches with the lantern raised a little, his face turned away from the opening, and goes over the door from the top down. This is his trade, and the door doesn't hide much from him.

There's no lock on it and never was. A bronze ring on the outside serves for a pull, green like the hinges. The jamb has no catch, no wire and no pin-hole, and none of the joints along the threshold are loose. Through the gap, by the lantern, he can make out a bronze bracket on the inside of the jamb, the kind a drop-bar sits in. Its twin will be on the far side. The bracket is empty. Either nobody barred this door from within, or nobody could.

Ash has drifted along the threshold, fine and pale grey, like hearth-sweepings. The draught out of the gap has blown it into a little ridge against the stone. It lies on his side of the sill as well, a hand's width out into the passage.

The door is clean as far as Pell can tell. He winds Sefa's strip round his left hand, takes the ring in his right, and lifts as he pushes so the foot of the door won't drag. It swings inward, a hand's width and then another. The bronze turns stiffly, with one low dry note as the hinges give, and then it opens without a scrape.

Sefa raises her wrapped hand and touches two fingers to the fleece. Light wells up in the wool, steady and white like a candle behind horn, and spreads out from her fist into the doorway.

At her hip, the Book of Shadows goes warm. It isn't much, only the warmth of a hand laid flat against her through the leather. It comes as the door opens and it stays.

---

The room is longer than it is wide, perhaps forty feet deep and half that across. The shelves Kesh described run back in rows from the door, dark oak twice Bram's height, and every shelf is full. The books still stand in their places, spines out, packed shoulder to shoulder. They're all black. They look like logs pulled whole out of a fire: they've kept their shape, but every edge is crazed and soft-looking, and nobody would want to breathe near them.

Ash covers the floor an inch deep, grey and level as new snow, except where it has been walked through.

There's one line of tracks. Hobnailed boots go in from the threshold, wavering, and one foot drags in a long furrow, step after step, down the middle aisle between the shelves.

Something lies in the ash just inside the door, a pace to the left, where a man would drop whatever he held to free his hand for the door. It's a chisel as long as a forearm, of black iron gone dull, with a mushroomed striking head. Nothing has settled on it. Its own fall has thrown the ash back from it in a little ring.

The tracks go on past the reach of Sefa's light and Pell's lantern, to the far end of the room.

Down there, between the last two shelves and low near the floor, there's another light. It's faint, no stronger than a coal seen through a cupped hand, and it isn't the colour of Sefa's or of the lantern. It's paler than either, a white-gold, like the top step at evening. At the very edge of where the light gives out, two boot soles are turned toward the door, their hobnails catching a gleam.

Nothing moves in there, and no ash stirs.

Out on the slope, in front of Bram's shield, the orange glow stays where it is, and nothing comes up the passage.

*(Out of game: no roll was needed. Checking a door is Pell's own trade, and there was nothing hidden on it to find.)*

 *(Out of game: none of Bram's four actions needed a roll, because nothing about them was uncertain: taking position, two orders and a standing rule. The turn already shows each one: he set himself facing out with his shield across him, gave Pell and Sefa their orders, and stated the "two in, one at the door" rule.)*
````
