At the very edge of where the light gives out, two boot soles are turned toward the door, their hobnails catching a gleam.

````text
Nothing moves in there, and no ash stirs.

Out on the slope, in front of Bram's shield, the orange glow stays where it is, and nothing comes up the passage.

*(Out of game: no roll was needed. Checking a door is your own trade, and there was nothing hidden on it to find.)*
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 15

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 15 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: You stand alone at the black door, facing down the slope. The cool air from the room at your back slides around your neck and along the top of your mail, and the longer you stand there the more it feels like standing with your back to a well. Out on the passage the heat presses at you from the front. Two breaths, two seasons, with your shield between them.

The lantern behind you shifts. Its light is low and inside the room now. You hear Pell move a board or two with his boot, and a soft dusty rustle that isn't the goose or the broom. Once Sefa murmurs something you don't catch, and then goes quiet again. The room behind you is as quiet as a hollow.

Down the slope, nothing changes. The orange glow lies on the walls in the middle distance and the hum comes up through your boots, steady as a vein.

Then, from inside the room, Pell says your name. Not loud. But in the way a man says it when he wants you to turn around and look.

"Bram. You'll want to see this." A pause. "Bring the shield."

Where you are: at the black door, facing down the slope · Time: Day 1 · afternoon

Sefa Marrin: Sefa keeps her glowing fist low, angled at the floor, and doesn't step in yet. She speaks over her shoulder in a breath.

"Chisel's just inside, on the left. Nobody touch it, nobody kick it. It's the one thing in here that could ring." A pause. "Tracks go all the way to the back. There's a light down there, white-gold, like the lamp Kesh drew. And boots. I think he's still here."

She lays her free hand flat on the Book at her hip for a moment. "And this went warm when the door opened. It hasn't done that before. I don't know yet if that's good."

She pulls the loose end of her fleece up over her nose and mouth and ties it off behind her head, rank as it is. Better goat than ash in the lungs.

"Bram, door, facing out, as you said. Pell, with me, two paces behind. Walk in his prints, in the furrow where the ash is already broken. Don't brush the shelves. If anything falls, let it fall."

Then she goes in, slowly, setting each boot down inside the dead man's hobnail tracks. She gives the chisel a wide berth on her right and keeps her light on the floor ahead and the shelf-ends on either side. She's watching for anything that isn't ash and black books: writing on the shelves or the floor, a second set of tracks, anything that moved since.

Halfway down the aisle she stops and calls softly toward the far end, the way she'd speak to a patient whose name she didn't know yet:

"Peace to you. I'm Sefa, from the hospice in the Lower City. Kesh heard you calling, two nights. I'm sorry no one came sooner."

If nothing answers, she walks the rest of the way. At the last two shelves she crouches beside the boots, still not touching anything. She looks first at the man: is he dead, and how long? What's in his hand, or near it, giving off that light? Is there anything written or scratched in the ash or on the wall where he lay? If there are words, she reads them before she does anything else.

Pell Dunmore: “Chisel just inside, left. Boots at the far end—and a light beside them.”

I pull a clean fold of cloth over my nose and mouth and step across the threshold, placing my feet carefully in the existing tracks so I stir as little ash as possible. I leave the chisel untouched.

“Sefa, come with me. Nothing bare-handed, and let’s leave that light where it is until we know what we’re looking at.”

With the lantern held forward, I follow the tracks slowly toward the boots, watching the floor and shelves for movement or anything else among the ash. I stop short of the figure to examine it and the white-gold light without touching either.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram holds the black door with his back to the cool and his face to the heat, shield across his body. Behind him the lantern-light moves off into the room and grows smaller. He can hear boots setting down slowly in old ash, a soft step and then a pause. Down the slope the orange glow stays where it is on the walls, and the hum comes up through his soles the same as before. Nothing climbs the passage, and no broom can be heard.

*(Out of game: Pell's call to Bram in Bram's paragraph is Pell's player's to make, so Bram holds the door facing out until someone inside actually calls him.)*

---

Sefa pulls the loose end of her fleece up over her nose and mouth and ties it behind her head. It's rank, and it's better than ash. She gives her orders over her shoulder in a breath: the chisel is the one thing in here that could ring, so nobody touches it, nobody brushes a shelf, and if anything falls they let it fall. Then she goes in.

She steps wide of the chisel on her right and sets her boots down inside the dead man's prints, in the furrow where the ash is already broken. Pell comes two paces behind her with a fold of cloth over his face and the lantern held forward and low. He puts his feet where hers have been. "Nothing bare-handed," he says quietly, "and that light stays where it is until we know what it is."

The air in here is cold enough to raise the skin on their arms after the slope. It's still. Their steps make no echo, as if the shelves soak up the sound. The black books stand packed on both sides, as tall as two men, and in Sefa's light their spines look soft as wet bread. Halfway down a crumb of char drops from a high shelf without anyone touching it and lands in the ash with no sound at all. Nobody stops for it.

She watches the floor as she goes. There is one line of tracks and no second set: no claw-prints, no paw-marks, no sweep-arcs. Nothing has crossed this ash since the hobnails did. Every shelf-end on both sides is bare, with no writing on any of them.

Halfway down the aisle she stops and speaks toward the far end, low, the way you speak to someone whose name you haven't learned yet.

"Peace to you. I'm Sefa, from the hospice in the Lower City. Kesh heard you calling, two nights. I'm sorry no one came sooner."

Nothing answers her. She walks the rest of the way.

---

He sits against the back wall between the last two shelves with his legs out toward the door, which is why the boot soles showed first. His head has fallen forward onto his chest. Ash has settled on his hair and shoulders and on the folds of a buff leather coat, like grey snow on a fence-post.

He was a big man, past forty, with a short beard gone stiff. The left leg is the one that dragged. The boot on that side is burned through at the ankle, and the breeches above it are charred to the knee and stuck to what's under them. Sefa has seen burns like that on the ward. They don't kill you at once. A waterskin lies beside his hip, flat and dry.

She doesn't need to touch him to know the rest, any more than she would at a bedside. He's dead, and he has been for some while. The cold, dry air has dried him instead of rotting him, the skin of his face is drawn tight and brown over the bones, and there's hardly any smell. He's been dead about a tenday, give or take a few days. It wasn't quick. A burn like that and no water takes about as long as Kesh said: two nights.

His right hand is closed into a fist against his breastbone. The light comes from there, leaking out between the fingers, white-gold and steady, like the top step at evening. In the cracks of his knuckles the skin is black, iron-black, the mark Kesh looked for on Sefa's hands. Ash lies everywhere else on him, but none has settled on that fist. It looks clean, as if he'd closed it a moment ago.

A fold of paper stands out of the breast of his coat above the fist, its edge grey with ash. From where she crouches Sefa can see its outer face, and there's nothing written on it, only a blob of dark red wax with the press of a seal she can't make out at this angle.

As she crouches, the Book at her hip gets warmer. It isn't much more, but it's more than it was at the door, and it keeps growing for as long as she stays near that hand.

Beside his left hand the ash has been scraped away down to the stone in a patch the size of a shield. A hobnail lies in the middle of it, worked loose from the burned boot and used as a pen. Scratched into the stone in rough, shaky capitals, in plain Common that Pell can read as well as Sefa:

‖ ‖

*DELLMERE PAYS ON DELIVERY.*
*NOBODY DELIVERS.*

*DONT LET IT GO OUT OF*

The last line stops there. The scratch of the final letter trails off down the stone and goes nowhere.

---

Pell stops a pace short of the boots with the lantern held low, and he looks without touching, the way he said he would. The floor round the man is undisturbed apart from the cleared patch and the man's own furrow. Nothing has been moved since he stopped moving. There's no wire, no trick in it. It's a dead man in a cold room with his hand shut on a light.

Behind them, at the far end of the aisle, the doorway is a dim rectangle of passage-dark with Bram's shoulder and the edge of his shield in it, facing away.
````

## Round 16

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 16 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram doesn't turn. He keeps his eyes on the orange glow and his voice pitched back through the open door, just loud enough for them to hear.

"If that white-gold is the lamp's kin, then Arren's warning stands: nothing of the fire goes up. We look, we learn, we don't carry it." He shifts his grip on the shield. "Keep clear of the chisel and the body until you've looked at both with the light low. If he's been dead since before the Warm One came, his boots may be the only honest thing in here."

He pauses, listening for the hum outside, then adds: "Call if that light brightens, or if the boots aren't empty. And Sefa—if your book goes warm again, you tell me, not after."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa stays crouched. Before anything else she bows her head, touches her beads with her free hand, and says the hospice words over him again, very low. They're the same ones she said over the basket of jaws. Then she pulls the fleece down from her mouth just far enough to speak to Pell.

"He's been dead about a tenday. The burn on that leg, and no water. Two nights, the way Kesh said." She points at the scratched stone without touching it, and reads it to him under her breath. "*Dellmere pays on delivery. Nobody delivers. Don't let it go out of...* and then it stops." She looks at the clean fist and the light leaking between his fingers. "Out of what? His hand? This room? I don't know. But he spent his last strength writing it, so we listen to him before we do anything clever."

She nods at the fold of paper in his coat. "Pell, that letter's yours. Wrapped hand, slow. See if the seal's whole and what's pressed in it. Don't break it yet, just ease it out if it comes without tearing. Dellmere's a name, and someone paid for that chisel."

Then she turns back to the hand. She doesn't reach for it at once. She holds her wrapped fist a hand's width above his and watches the Book at her hip and the light between his fingers. Does either change? Does the white-gold lean toward her own Light, or shy from it? She looks at it the way she was taught to look at a wound before dressing it, and lets everything she knows of the arcane and the Faith tell her what she's seeing. Is that a piece of the lamp's flame, or something else?

"If it's what I think," she murmurs, "it was safe here because he can't see in here. The moment it's in the passage, it may be the brightest thing the Warm One has felt in years." A breath. "So we look at it here, and we decide here."

If nothing she sees warns her off, she lays her fleece-wrapped fingers over his and starts to ease them open, one at a time and very gently, the way you uncurl a dead hand on the ward so the family won't see it clenched. She's ready to close her own wrapped palm over whatever's inside, so that not a drop of it falls into the ash. Or goes out.

Pell Dunmore: I crouch beside Sefa, careful not to disturb his fist.

“‘Delivery.’ Someone hired them to take something away. Dellmere—does that name mean anything to you?”

I angle the lantern toward the paper in his coat. “I’ll try the note. Leave his hand as it is for now.”

With my fleece-wrapped fingers, I gently test the exposed edge of the paper. If it holds together, I ease it free and unfold it with support beneath it; if it starts to crumble, I stop.

I keep my voice low. “That last line could mean out of the room, out of the cellar—or out of his hand. Best not guess.”
````

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Arcana: reading the light in the dead man's fist before it is uncovered · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Sefa Marrin · Arcana: reading the light in the dead man's fist before it is uncovered · decide · presses: [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram keeps his back to the cold room and his face to the heat. His voice comes down the aisle low and even, and it reaches the far end because nothing else in the room makes a sound: look and learn, don't carry it, keep clear of the chisel and the body until they've been seen with the light low, call if the light brightens. Then he adds the order for Sefa: if the Book goes warm again, she tells him at once, not afterwards. After that he says nothing more. Out on the slope the orange stays where it is on the walls.

At the back wall Sefa bows her head over the dead man and says the hospice words a second time, the same short ones she said over the basket of jaws. She keeps her beads in her left hand. Then she pulls the fleece down off her mouth far enough to talk to Pell. About a tenday dead, she tells him. It was the leg and no water, two nights, the way Kesh said. She reads the scratches out under her breath, *Dellmere pays on delivery. Nobody delivers. Don't let it go out of*, and leaves the end of the line hanging the way he did.

Then she holds her wrapped right fist a hand's width above his fist and watches.

The two lights don't mix the way two lamps would. Her Light is white and plain, a candle behind horn. The light between his fingers is warmer, white-gold, and it doesn't lean toward hers or shrink away. It goes on as if hers weren't there. The Book against her hip is warmer than it was at the door. It's the warmth of a cup held through cloth now, and it stays at that.

She looks at it the way she was taught to look at a wound before she dresses it, and works through what she knows of wards and of the Faith.

She can read it, and the reading is plain.

This isn't a light somebody cast to see by. There's no evocation in it and no wizard's flourish. It's warding, the sort that's laid on a threshold to hold something back, and it's whole. A spent charm gutters and fails, and this does neither. It burns steadily, as if it were still doing its work, like a link cut from a chain that hasn't noticed the chain has gone. That's why no ash will settle on the fist: the light pushes it away, gently and all the time, as a ward pushes away what it was made to keep out.

There's a second thing under that, and Sefa knows it the way she'd know her own handwriting. The light itself isn't a wizard's light. The school only shaped it. Underneath, it's the same thing that answered her on the ninth night of the fever. It's lent light, and it came from the same well as hers.

Nothing she sees warns her off.

She lays her wrapped fingers over his. The skin of his hand is dry and tight, like a glove left too long by a fire. She eases the first finger up, slowly, the way the sisters taught her to uncurl a hand on the ward so the family wouldn't see it clenched. It gives. The second gives too. At the third a knuckle lets go with a small dry crack, and she keeps going without hurrying. Her own palm is cupped under his the whole time.

The light comes out into the room.

It's a shard of pale brass, about the size of her palm, shaped like a wedge where it broke away from something bigger. Two of its edges are bright and raw, bitten with the square marks of a chisel. The face turned up toward her carries a tiny lantern, engraved no bigger than her thumbnail, with a ring handle and a single drop of flame. The glow comes from that drop. The break cuts right through the bottom of it, a chipped tooth, the way Kesh drew it in the air. As his fingers fall open the shard tips into her wrapped palm. She closes her hand over it before it can touch the ash.

It's warm, like a hand that has just let go of hers. In the cup of her fist the white-gold leaks out through the wool beside her own white Light. In the uncovered air for those few moments, it spread a pale wash about ten feet round them. It lit the spines of the nearest black books and the dead man's face and Pell's knees, and nothing more. The Book at her hip and the shard in her hand feel the same warm. It's like being held by two hands that belong to one person.

---

Pell crouches at her other side with the lantern turned toward the dead man's coat. "*Delivery.* Someone hired them to fetch something out. Dellmere. Does that mean anything to you?" Then, already reaching for the paper with his wrapped hand: "I'll try the note. Leave his hand as it is for now."

His words land with the fingers already open and the shard already in Sefa's fist.

The paper isn't ash. It's good heavy rag paper that has only been sitting in a dead man's coat, and it slides free when he tests the edge. The wax was broken a long time ago. The red seal split cleanly along the fold when someone opened it the first time, and the two halves still stick to either side. Pressed into the wax is a merchant's mark: an anvil with a tall D cut across its face.

Pell knows that mark. He's seen it stencilled on iron bales on the wharves of the Gray Harbor and painted over a warehouse door down there. Dellmere is a middling iron-factoring house of the Gate. He never took a job from them, and they never had a reason to know his name.

He opens it on his flat palm, keeping his other hand underneath it, and holds the lantern so Sefa can read it with him.

> *Dask —*
>
> *Terms as agreed. Forty gold on your hand tonight, the balance of one hundred and sixty on delivery, at this house or at our office on the Gray Harbor.*
>
> *East of the Ulcaster stones, the warm hill. A stair under it, and at its foot a passage to the lower cellar. You will find a door with a lamp on it. The lamp is the lock. The chisel will answer for it.*
>
> *Open the lower cellar and bring out the book bound in iron. That, and nothing else.*
>
> *The beast below may be left to itself. Do not trouble with it.*
>
> *Burn this.*
>
> *— O. Tarr, for Dellmere Iron Factors*

He didn't burn it.

At the top, above *Dask*, a fold-line has worn through, and in the gap someone has written in pencil, smaller and later, *at the Burning Wizard, Beregost*. That's the inn on the main street there, where the caravan men drink.

"That last line could mean out of the room, out of the cellar, or out of his hand," Pell says, low, looking from the scratches on the stone to Sefa's closed fist. "Best not guess."

Under the open coat a flat leather purse hangs on the dead man's belt, buckled shut. Nobody has touched it.

The room is as still as before. Down the length of the aisle Bram's shoulder and the rim of his shield stand in the doorway, facing out, and the white-gold in Sefa's fist is far too small to reach him.

 *(Out of game: none of these four needed a roll, since nothing about them was uncertain, and all four already happen in the scene above. Sefa says the hospice words a second time. She eases the fingers open and catches the shard in her wrapped palm. She reads the scratches to Pell under her breath. Pell eases the letter free and finds the anvil-and-D seal already broken along the fold.)*
````

Rolls: Sefa Marrin Arcana: reading the light in the dead man's fist before it is uncovered 19 vs 15 success

**Bram Halloway's screen (the relay):**

````text
Behind you the cold room stays quiet. Your own voice carries down the aisle low and even, and it reaches the far end because nothing else in there makes a sound. After that you say nothing more.

From the back wall, far down the aisle, come low voices: the two of them murmuring over the dead man, too quiet to make out from the doorway. Nobody calls to you. The light doesn't brighten, and nobody says anything about the boots.

Out on the slope the orange stays where it is on the walls. You stand in the doorway with your shoulder and the rim of your shield facing out, and the room at your back stays as still as before.
````

**Sefa Marrin's screen (the relay):**

````text
She can read it, and the reading is plain.

This isn't a light somebody cast to see by. There's no evocation in it and no wizard's flourish. It's warding, the sort that's laid on a threshold to hold something back, and it's whole. A spent charm gutters and fails, and this does neither. It burns steadily, as if it were still doing its work, like a link cut from a chain that hasn't noticed the chain has gone. That's why no ash will settle on the fist: the light pushes it away, gently and all the time, as a ward pushes away what it was made to keep out.

There's a second thing under that, and you know it the way you'd know your own handwriting. The light itself isn't a wizard's light. The school only shaped it. Underneath, it's the same thing that answered you on the ninth night of the fever. It's lent light, and it came from the same well as yours.

Nothing you see warns you off.

You lay your wrapped fingers over his. The skin of his hand is dry and tight, like a glove left too long by a fire. You ease the first finger up, slowly, the way the sisters taught you to uncurl a hand on the ward so the family wouldn't see it clenched. It gives. The second gives too. At the third a knuckle lets go with a small dry crack, and you keep going without hurrying. Your own palm is cupped under his the whole time.

The light comes out into the room.

It's a shard of pale brass, about the size of your palm, shaped like a wedge where it broke away from something bigger. Two of its edges are bright and raw, bitten with the square marks of a chisel. The face turned up toward you carries a tiny lantern, engraved no bigger than your thumbnail, with a ring handle and a single drop of flame. The glow comes from that drop. The break cuts right through the bottom of it, a chipped tooth, the way Kesh drew it in the air. As his fingers fall open the shard tips into your wrapped palm. You close your hand over it before it can touch the ash.

It's warm, like a hand that has just let go of yours. In the cup of your fist the white-gold leaks out through the wool beside your own white Light. In the uncovered air for those few moments, it spread a pale wash about ten feet round you. It lit the spines of the nearest black books and the dead man's face and Pell's knees, and nothing more. The Book at your hip and the shard in your hand feel the same warm. It's like being held by two hands that belong to one person.

Pell's words reached you with the fingers already open and the shard already in your fist.

The paper isn't ash. It's good heavy rag paper that has only been sitting in a dead man's coat, and it slides free when he tests the edge. The wax was broken a long time ago. The red seal split cleanly along the fold when someone opened it the first time, and the two halves still stick to either side. Pressed into the wax is a merchant's mark: an anvil with a tall D cut across its face.

He opens it on his flat palm, keeping his other hand underneath it, and holds the lantern so you can read it with him.

> *Dask —*
>
> *Terms as agreed. Forty gold on your hand tonight, the balance of one hundred and sixty on delivery, at this house or at our office on the Gray Harbor.*
>
> *East of the Ulcaster stones, the warm hill. A stair under it, and at its foot a passage to the lower cellar. You will find a door with a lamp on it. The lamp is the lock. The chisel will answer for it.*
>
> *Open the lower cellar and bring out the book bound in iron. That, and nothing else.*
>
> *The beast below may be left to itself. Do not trouble with it.*
>
> *Burn this.*
>
> *— O. Tarr, for Dellmere Iron Factors*

He didn't burn it.

At the top, above *Dask*, a fold-line has worn through, and in the gap someone has written in pencil, smaller and later, *at the Burning Wizard, Beregost*.

As Pell speaks he looks from the scratches on the stone to your closed fist.

Under the open coat a flat leather purse hangs on the dead man's belt, buckled shut. Nobody has touched it.

The room is as still as before. Down the length of the aisle Bram's shoulder and the rim of his shield stand in the doorway, facing out, and the white-gold in your fist is far too small to reach him.
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Bram keeps his back to the cold room and his face to the heat. His voice comes down the aisle low and even, and it reaches the far end because nothing else in the room makes a sound: "If that white-gold is the lamp's kin, then Arren's warning stands: nothing of the fire goes up. We look, we learn, we don't carry it." He shifts his grip on the shield. "Keep clear of the chisel and the body until you've looked at both with the light low. If he's been dead since before the Warm One came, his boots may be the only honest thing in here." He pauses, then adds: "Call if that light brightens, or if the boots aren't empty. And Sefa, if your book goes warm again, you tell me, not after." After that he says nothing more. Out on the slope the orange stays where it is on the walls.

At the back wall Sefa bows her head over the dead man and says the hospice words a second time, very low, the same short ones she said over the basket of jaws. She keeps her beads in her left hand. Then she pulls the fleece down off her mouth far enough to talk to you.

"He's been dead about a tenday. The burn on that leg, and no water. Two nights, the way Kesh said." She points at the scratched stone without touching it and reads it to you under her breath: "*Dellmere pays on delivery. Nobody delivers. Don't let it go out of...* and then it stops." She looks at the clean fist and the light leaking between his fingers. "Out of what? His hand? This room? I don't know. But he spent his last strength writing it, so we listen to him before we do anything clever."

She nods at the fold of paper in his coat. "Pell, that letter's yours. Wrapped hand, slow. See if the seal's whole and what's pressed in it. Don't break it yet, just ease it out if it comes without tearing. Dellmere's a name, and someone paid for that chisel."

Then she holds her wrapped right fist a hand's width above his fist and watches. Her Light is white and plain, a candle behind horn. The light between his fingers is warmer, white-gold. It doesn't lean toward hers or shrink away. It goes on as if hers weren't there.

"If it's what I think," she murmurs, "it was safe here because he can't see in here. The moment it's in the passage, it may be the brightest thing the Warm One has felt in years." A breath. "So we look at it here, and we decide here."

She lays her wrapped fingers over his and eases the first finger up, slowly. It gives. The second gives too. At the third a knuckle lets go with a small dry crack, and she keeps going without hurrying. Her own palm is cupped under his the whole time.

The light comes out into the room.

It's a shard of pale brass, about the size of her palm, shaped like a wedge where it broke away from something bigger. Two of its edges are bright and raw, bitten with the square marks of a chisel. The face turned up carries a tiny engraved lantern, no bigger than a thumbnail, with a ring handle and a single drop of flame. The glow comes from that drop. The break cuts right through the bottom of it, a chipped tooth, the way Kesh drew it in the air. As his fingers fall open the shard tips into her wrapped palm, and she closes her hand over it before it can touch the ash.

For those few moments in the uncovered air it spread a pale wash about ten feet round you both. It lit the spines of the nearest black books, the dead man's face and your knees, and nothing more. Now the white-gold leaks out through the wool of her fist beside her own white Light.

Your words land with the fingers already open and the shard already in her fist.

The paper isn't ash. It's good heavy rag paper that has only been sitting in a dead man's coat, and it slides free when you test the edge. The wax was broken a long time ago. The red seal split cleanly along the fold when someone opened it the first time, and the two halves still stick to either side. Pressed into the wax is a merchant's mark: an anvil with a tall D cut across its face.

You know that mark. You've seen it stencilled on iron bales on the wharves of the Gray Harbor and painted over a warehouse door down there. Dellmere is a middling iron-factoring house of the Gate. You never took a job from them, and they never had a reason to know your name.

You open it on your flat palm, keeping your other hand underneath it, and hold the lantern so Sefa can read it with you.

> *Dask —*
>
> *Terms as agreed. Forty gold on your hand tonight, the balance of one hundred and sixty on delivery, at this house or at our office on the Gray Harbor.*
>
> *East of the Ulcaster stones, the warm hill. A stair under it, and at its foot a passage to the lower cellar. You will find a door with a lamp on it. The lamp is the lock. The chisel will answer for it.*
>
> *Open the lower cellar and bring out the book bound in iron. That, and nothing else.*
>
> *The beast below may be left to itself. Do not trouble with it.*
>
> *Burn this.*
>
> *— O. Tarr, for Dellmere Iron Factors*

He didn't burn it.

At the top, above *Dask*, a fold-line has worn through, and in the gap someone has written in pencil, smaller and later, *at the Burning Wizard, Beregost*. That's the inn on the main street there, where the caravan men drink.

As you speak, you look from the scratches on the stone to Sefa's closed fist.

Under the open coat a flat leather purse hangs on the dead man's belt, buckled shut. Nobody has touched it.

The room is as still as before. Down the length of the aisle Bram's shoulder and the rim of his shield stand in the doorway, facing out, and the white-gold in Sefa's fist is far too small to reach him.
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 17

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 17 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram plants his feet and lets his eyes travel the slope while two sounds run under him: the deep unlikely quiet behind, the far reach of the warm air moving out at his back like the house itself breathing through him. The orange glow is a long way off; the walls catch it high on the left side, and below that all is dark. Nothing climbs the passage, and nobody sweeps.

He stands in the doorway as he promised he would. Heat folds over him from the front, and the cool from the room slides past his hips toward the slope. Sweat runs down the side of his face and soaks into the fleece at his throat.

He can taste the ash in the air behind him now, dry and old as a cold hearth, with a faint bitterness under it like spent charcoal. The wool over his face is rank, but it's easier not to think about that. He's smelled worse.

The doorway itself is broad enough for two men to pass. Inside is a long stone room, lit far off by pale gold that moves. He glimpses it only at the edge of his eye, across his shoulder, little as a candle flame but white-gold and unnervingly still, over by the far wall. Sound carries down the aisle weirdly, soft and clean, the way voices do over water.

There, by the jamb on his left, flat in the ash, is the chisel Sefa spoke of. It lies in a burned furrow as if it had been dropped or kicked aside, not thrown—black iron, cold-looking, about as long as a man's forearm, with its edge turned toward the wall. It doesn't glint. It drinks the light from the room rather than giving any back. The tang is wrapped with leather turned grey and split. Where the iron shows, the metal is pitted like something drawn up from a well.

He doesn't move toward it. He stays in the doorway, facing out, listening to the two voices down at the far end.

They are moving now. Boot-steps, slow, and the wet drag of a body being rolled or lifted, then a grunt. The light goes on burning beyond them, so still it hurts a little behind the eyes if you look too long.

Then Pell's voice:

"Bram. We're coming back. Sefa has the mace. Get ready to walk." A pause, then closer and sharper: "There's no honour in this room. We're leaving it as we found it."

He can hear them coming up the aisle behind him, slow, bearing something between them.

His turn. What he's doing at his back he may not turn to see clearly without leaving the door; he's kept his eyes outward as ordered.

Sefa Marrin: Sefa keeps her fist shut tight over the shard and lets out a long breath through the fleece. For a moment she just holds it, eyes closed, the way you'd hold the hand of someone who has finally stopped hurting.

Then she turns her head toward the door and pitches her voice so it carries down the aisle and no further.

"Bram. You said tell you, not after. The Book's warm, like a cup through cloth. It went warmer when I came near his hand. I have the light. It's a piece of the lamp, brass, chiselled off. A ward-link, still whole, still working, and it's the same light as mine. The same well." She swallows. "Nothing's moved in here. He's dead, a tenday, the leg and no water. Stay where you are."

She turns back to Pell and reads the letter over his hands a second time, slowly, her lips moving at *the lamp is the lock* and *the beast below may be left to itself*.

"So that's what this was," she breathes. "Dellmere didn't want the Warm One. They wanted a book bound in iron, from behind the door. To them he's a nuisance in the way. *Leave it to itself.*" Her jaw tightens. "They sent four men to cut the lock off a dragon's cage for a book, and didn't care what walked out after. Dask figured it out at the end. *Nobody delivers.*"

She nods at the fold-line. "The Burning Wizard, Beregost. Fold that up and keep it close, Pell. Somebody named O. Tarr owes these men an answer, and Dellmere still wants that book. They'll send others."

Then she looks down at her closed fist.

"Here's what I think, and stop me if I'm wrong. This room's the one place he can't see. The moment this leaves it, it's the brightest thing in the cellar. Kesh told me not to show it to him, not first. And I promised her I'd come up and tell her what I saw before I did anything at the lamp." She turns the fist slightly. "So it stays here, with Dask, for now. He kept it safe two nights with his last strength. He can keep it a little longer."

She works carefully. She folds the rest of her hand-wrap strip round the shard until no light leaks out at all, and lays the bundle gently back into the dead man's open palm, then closes his stiff fingers over it again, no harder than they'll go.

"*Don't let it go out*," she murmurs to him. "We won't. Not out of this room till it goes home."

She glances at the purse and shakes her head at Pell. "Leave his coin. If there's anything in it with a name on it, a token, a letter for kin, look, but put it back. When this is done, he gets proper rites. Not a basket."

She rises, wiping her wrapped hand on her knee, and keeps her light low on the floor.

"Then we go to the door and look at the lamp with our own eyes. We read Arren's words under it, we hear the Warm One out, and we say our terms to his face. No hands on anything. After that I go up to Kesh, and then we come back here for the shard." A thin smile behind the fleece. "And if he asks what we found in the wardens' room, I'll tell him the truth: a dead man who didn't listen to anyone either."

She starts back down the aisle in Dask's prints, wide of the chisel, toward Bram's shoulder in the doorway.

Pell Dunmore: “Dellmere. I know the mark—iron factors back in the Gate.” I fold the letter carefully and put it away. “They wanted an iron-bound book out of the lower cellar. The beast was incidental. Two hundred gold to break a lock they knew was a lamp.”

I glance at the shard in Sefa’s hand. “Well, now we know what they broke off. Keep it covered here until we’ve agreed how to return it.”

I call softly down the aisle to Bram: “He died about a tenday ago. We found the commission: Dellmere hired them to break the lamp and fetch an iron-bound book. Sefa has the missing piece. We’re still deciding here.”

Then I examine the dead man’s purse and its buckle. If there’s nothing suspicious, I open it with my wrapped hands, keeping it supported over his lap.

“Sorry, Dask. You kept the letter. Let’s see if you kept anything else that explains this.”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram holds the doorway with his shield across his body and the slope in front of him. Heat presses on his face from below, and the cool air of the room slides past the backs of his knees and out into the passage. The lantern has gone in with Pell, so the doorway is dim. The only light out here is the orange, high on the left-hand wall forty feet down, and whatever spills past his shoulder from the room. Sound reaches him from behind oddly, soft and close, with no echo, as if the shelves were swallowing it.

Sefa's voice comes up the aisle, pitched to reach him and go no farther. He told her to tell him and not after, so she does. The Book is warm, like a cup held through cloth, and it got warmer as she came near the dead man's hand. She has the light. It's a piece of the lamp, brass, cut off with a chisel. It's a ward-link, still whole and still working, and it's the same light as hers. Nothing in the room has moved. The man has been dead a tenday, from the leg and no water. Stay where you are.

He stays where he is. Down the slope nothing climbs.

*(Out of game: Bram's paragraph again voiced Pell's call and added things that aren't in the room, a mace and a body being lifted, so I've played Bram holding the door and listening, and used Pell's and Sefa's own words.)*

---

At the back wall Sefa keeps her fist shut and lets out a long breath through the fleece. For a moment she just holds it there, eyes closed.

Then she reads the letter a second time over Pell's flat palm, slowly. Her lips move at *the lamp is the lock* and again at *the beast below may be left to itself*. She says it low: Dellmere wanted the book and never cared about the Warm One. They sent four men to cut the lock off his cage and didn't care what walked out after, and Dask understood that at the end. *Nobody delivers.* She tells Pell to fold the letter and keep it close.

Then she unwinds the strip from her hand.

Her Light comes away with it. The glow was laid on the wool, not on her, and as the last turn comes off her bare fingers they go dark. Only Pell's lantern lights her now. She opens her fist over the loose fleece and the shard lies in the white glow. As she turns it to fold the wool over, its other face comes up for the first time.

There's a tower cut into the brass, worn but plain: tall and narrow, with a squared crown and three slit windows down its face. It's the same sign as on the lintel at the mouth, and on the waystones Bram has passed a hundred times on the east road. Ulcaster's tower is on one face and the lantern on the other. She folds the wool over both, and the white-gold disappears.

The bundle doesn't go dark, though. The wool is still carrying her Light, white and steady like a candle behind horn. When she lays it in Dask's open palm and closes his stiff fingers over it, as far as they'll go, light shows between his knuckles again. It's whiter now than the shard's was.

"*Don't let it go out*," she murmurs to him. "We won't. Not out of this room till it goes home."

When she straightens and steps back, the warmth at her hip drops with every pace. The cup through cloth fades to a hand laid flat, the way it was at the door, and stays there.

She tells Pell to leave the man's coin. If there's anything in the purse with a name on it, a token or a letter for kin, he can look, but he puts it back. Dask gets proper rites when this is done, not a basket. Then she starts back up the aisle in Dask's prints, keeping wide of the chisel, toward the dim shape of Bram's shield in the doorway.

*(Out of game: Sefa's Light is on that strip, so it will keep shining in Dask's hand for the rest of its hour, about half an hour more, unless she ends it. The shard's own glow doesn't show through.)*

---

"Dellmere. I know the mark. Iron factors back in the Gate." Pell folds the letter along its old creases and slips it inside his jerkin. "They wanted an iron-bound book out of the lower cellar. The beast was incidental. Two hundred gold to break a lock they knew was a lamp."

He watches the bundle go into the dead man's hand. "Well, now we know what they broke off. Keep it covered here until we've agreed how to return it." It's already covered, and he gives it one short nod.

He calls softly up the aisle: dead about a tenday, the commission found, Dellmere hired them to break the lamp and fetch an iron-bound book, Sefa has the missing piece, they're still deciding. At the door Bram hears it the same way he heard Sefa, close and with no echo.

Then Pell turns to the purse.

He checks the buckle first. It's a plain brass tongue, stiff with old sweat. There's no needle, no second catch and no thread running anywhere it shouldn't. He works it loose with his wrapped hand and holds the purse open over the dead man's lap so nothing can spill into the ash.

"Sorry, Dask. You kept the letter. Let's see if you kept anything else that explains this."

There are twelve gold pieces and nothing else. No token, no folded note, nothing with a name on it.

The coins aren't Gate money. They're Amnian danters, the gold that comes up the Way from Athkatla, and Pell has handled enough coin to know them by weight and stamp in this light. They're new, too. The edges are still sharp from the mint and the faces show no wear. None of them has been clipped or bitten. If the letter is honest about forty in hand, this is what's left of the advance, and every coin looks as if it came out of the same bag on the same day.

He holds the purse open over Dask's knees.

---

Bram hears the broom first. *Shhk, shhk.* It's coming up out of the orange, quick.

A small shape comes up against the glow, dark against the lit wall, and stops ten feet below the door. Ixx doesn't come into the room's spill of light. He holds the broom crosswise across his body. His tail is wound twice round his ankle and his apron hangs scorched at the hem. Sefa comes up to Bram's shoulder from the dark behind him, her right hand bare and empty, just as the kobold starts to speak.

It's the recited voice, laid word on word, and there's less of Ixx in it than before.

"The Warm One says: *the wardens' room is ash. Ash says nothing worth the waiting.*"

He swallows.

"*The Warm One has been patient. He said it two times. He does not say it a third.* *The shield-man sets terms to faces. Bring the faces. All three, at the door. Now.*"

That's the end of it. Ixx's eyes go past Bram into the room, to where Pell's lantern makes a small yellow island at the far end with a whiter glow beside it, low down, and then come back. When he speaks again it's his own voice, small and fast.

"After the broom, he does not send the broom." A short wet hiss through his teeth. "He sends elders. Slings." He looks at Sefa. "Not little ones. Elders. Kesh too."

He grips the handle with both claws.

"Ixx sweeps you through the long room. Now?"

 *(Out of game: neither needed a roll, since standing your ground and leaving the chisel alone have no uncertainty to them. Both already happen in the scene above: Bram holds the doorway with his shield facing out, listening while the others work, and the chisel stays untouched in the ash just inside the door.)*
````

````text
He swallows.

"*The Warm One has been patient. He said it two times. He does not say it a third.* *The shield-man sets terms to faces. Bring the faces. All three, at the door. Now.*"

That's the end of it. Ixx's eyes go past Bram into the room, to where Pell's lantern makes a small yellow island at the far end with a whiter glow beside it, low down, and then come back. When he speaks again it's his own voice, small and fast.

"After the broom, he does not send the broom." A short wet hiss through his teeth. "He sends elders. Slings." He looks at you. "Not little ones. Elders. Kesh too."

He grips the handle with both claws.

"Ixx sweeps you through the long room. Now?"
````

**Pell Dunmore's screen (the relay):**

````text
Sefa keeps her fist shut over the shard and lets out a long breath through the fleece. For a moment she just holds it there, eyes closed. Then she turns her head toward the door and pitches her voice up the aisle: "Bram. You said tell you, not after. The Book's warm, like a cup through cloth. It went warmer when I came near his hand. I have the light. It's a piece of the lamp, brass, chiselled off. A ward-link, still whole, still working, and it's the same light as mine. The same well. Nothing's moved in here. He's dead, a tenday, the leg and no water. Stay where you are."

She reads the letter a second time over your flat palm, slowly. Her lips move at *the lamp is the lock* and again at *the beast below may be left to itself*. She says it low: Dellmere wanted the book and never cared about the Warm One. They sent four men to cut the lock off his cage and didn't care what walked out after, and Dask understood that at the end. *Nobody delivers.* She tells you to fold the letter and keep it close.

"Dellmere. I know the mark. Iron factors back in the Gate." You fold the letter along its old creases and slip it inside your jerkin.

Then she unwinds the strip from her hand. The glow comes away with it; it was laid on the wool, not on her, and as the last turn comes off her bare fingers they go dark. Only your lantern lights her now. She opens her fist over the loose fleece and the shard lies in the white glow. As she turns it to fold the wool over, its other face comes up for the first time.

There's a tower cut into the brass, worn but plain: tall and narrow, with a squared crown and three slit windows down its face. It's the same sign as on the lintel at the mouth, and on the waystones of the east road. Ulcaster's tower is on one face and the lantern on the other. She folds the wool over both, and the white-gold disappears.

The bundle doesn't go dark, though. The wool is still glowing, white and steady like a candle behind horn. When she lays it in Dask's open palm and closes his stiff fingers over it, as far as they'll go, light shows between his knuckles again. It's whiter now than the shard's was.

"*Don't let it go out*," she murmurs to him. "We won't. Not out of this room till it goes home."

You watch the bundle go into the dead man's hand. It's already covered, and you give it one short nod.

She tells you to leave the man's coin. If there's anything in the purse with a name on it, a token or a letter for kin, you can look, but you put it back. Dask gets proper rites when this is done, not a basket. Then she starts back up the aisle in Dask's prints, keeping wide of the chisel, toward the dim shape of Bram's shield in the doorway.

Your call up the aisle goes soft and close, with no echo.

Then you turn to the purse. You check the buckle first. It's a plain brass tongue, stiff with old sweat. There's no needle, no second catch and no thread running anywhere it shouldn't. You work it loose with your wrapped hand and hold the purse open over the dead man's lap so nothing can spill into the ash.

There are twelve gold pieces and nothing else. No token, no folded note, nothing with a name on it.

The coins aren't Gate money. They're Amnian danters, the gold that comes up the Way from Athkatla, and you've handled enough coin to know them by weight and stamp in this light. They're new, too. The edges are still sharp from the mint and the faces show no wear. None of them has been clipped or bitten. If the letter is honest about forty in hand, this is what's left of the advance, and every coin looks as if it came out of the same bag on the same day.

You hold the purse open over Dask's knees.

---

From the doorway comes the sound of a broom, *shhk, shhk*, quick, and then a voice beyond it, reaching you soft and close with no echo. It's Ixx's recited voice, laid word on word, with less of Ixx in it than before.

"The Warm One says: *the wardens' room is ash. Ash says nothing worth the waiting.*"

A pause.

"*The Warm One has been patient. He said it two times. He does not say it a third.* *The shield-man sets terms to faces. Bring the faces. All three, at the door. Now.*"

Then his own voice, small and fast: "After the broom, he does not send the broom. He sends elders. Slings. Not little ones. Elders. Kesh too."

And: "Ixx sweeps you through the long room. Now?"
````

Together after: [Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore]

## Round 18

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 18 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram keeps his shield toward the slope and his eyes on Ixx. "We're coming. But not at a sling's pace and not with the black door open at our backs." His words carry only to the kobold, then soften as he half-turns to Sefa and Pell. "Close it. Leave everything where it lies, including the chisel. I don't care if it's cold iron; it belongs to the house, not to us. Sefa, wrap that brass in wool and keep it close to your body, under your belt. If it warms again, I'll know."

Then he looks back to Ixx. "Tell the Warm One this: we're not men who can be fetched with stones. We said we'd come to his door, and we will—before the elders need to draw a single sling. You sweep. We walk. If Kesh or any elder steps onto the long floor with a stone in hand, I'll call it treachery and we'll go back up the stair." He lets that settle, then nods. "Now, Ixx. Lead, and don't stop."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa lifts two fingers toward the far end of the room and lets the Light go. Down by the back wall, the white between Dask's knuckles fades out, and only Pell's lantern is left. Nothing in there needs to shine for anyone now.

She crouches at the threshold so she's level with Ixx, but stays inside the doorway's spill. Her voice is calm and quick.

"We're coming, Ixx. Now. All three faces, at the door, the way he asked. Go ahead of us, or sweep us, whichever is faster. No need for slings. No need for Kesh." She meets his eyes. "Tell him that if you can get there first. *The faces are coming.*"

She stands and speaks low for Bram, barely a breath.

"He's done waiting, and he'll put the clan between us and him if we make him. I promised Kesh no trouble near the little ones. We go now and keep it at words." A beat. "The shard stays with Dask. If he asks what's in the wardens' room, it's a dead man and ash. That's true enough. We hear him, we read the lamp, we put our hands on nothing."

Then she calls softly back up the aisle:

"Pell. Leave the purse as it was and come. Walk his prints out, wide of the chisel. Pull the door to behind you, gently. Bronze, not iron. It won't ring."

While Pell comes she looks at Ixx again and keeps it short.

"Ixx, in the long room, how many holes sleep light? Walk us the slowest way, not the fastest. Bram's mail talks a little even wrapped." She takes her place at the back of the line, two steps behind Pell's lantern, hands bare and empty, the Book quiet against her hip.

"Sweep on. We step where you step."

Pell Dunmore: I close the purse and buckle it back onto Dask’s belt, leaving all twelve coins.

“New Amnian danters,” I murmur. “Same minting, by the look of them.”

I leave the wrapped shard in his hand and retrace the footprints to the doorway, lantern low.

Quietly to Bram and Sefa: “He can’t hear us in here. Before we answer: we mustn’t let him hurry us into breaking what’s left of that lock. We can hear him at the door without promising anything.”

Then, to Ixx, without stepping out past Bram: “We heard you. Does the Warm One know Kesh gave us the fleeces? If he sends her against us, can she refuse?”

I keep my voice level. “I don’t want anyone hurt over his impatience.”
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram keeps the shield toward the slope and his eyes on the small shape against the orange. He pitches his voice so it reaches the kobold and goes no farther. They're coming, he says, but not at a sling's pace, and not with the black door open behind them.

Ixx looks at the door when he hears that, and keeps looking a moment longer than he needs to. "Shut is good," he says. "Ixx likes it shut."

Then Bram gives him the message. They aren't men who can be fetched with stones. They said they'd come to the door and they will, before any elder has to draw a sling. If an elder steps onto the long floor with a stone in hand, Bram will call it treachery and they'll go back up the stair. Ixx's lips move over each piece as it comes. *Not fetched with stones. Before a sling.* At *treachery* they stop. He tries the word once under his breath, can't seem to find it anywhere, and lets it go.

"Faces come, he sends no elders," he says. "Elders are for faces that don't come." His shoulders drop a little as he says it.

---

Sefa stands at Bram's shoulder in the doorway. She raises two fingers toward the far end of the room and lets the Light go.

At the back wall the white between Dask's knuckles thins and goes out. Nothing shows through the wool after it. The only light left in the room is Pell's lantern, a small yellow island at the end of the aisle. Ixx sees the white go. He looks at the dark patch where it was, then quickly away.

Sefa crouches at the threshold, still inside the cool air spilling from the room, so her eyes are level with his ten feet down the slope. Her voice is calm and quick. They're coming now, all three faces at the door the way he asked, and there's no need for slings and no need for Kesh. He can go ahead or sweep them down, whichever is faster. *The faces are coming.*

Ixx shakes his head. "Feet are faster than Ixx." He points his snout down the slope toward the glow. "He hears feet on the slope. Feet coming, he knows before Ixx gets there. Ixx stays. Ixx sweeps."

Sefa stands again and speaks for Bram alone, barely a breath. They go now and keep it to words, because she promised Kesh no trouble near the little ones. The shard stays with Dask. If the Warm One asks what's in the wardens' room, it's a dead man and ash. They hear him, they read the lamp, and they put their hands on nothing.

Down the slope the warm air keeps coming up at its old slow pace. The hum under the floor stays as it was, and the orange stays where it is on the walls.

Sefa turns and calls softly up the aisle. Pell should leave the purse as he found it and come out in Dask's prints, wide of the chisel, and pull the door to behind him. It's bronze, not iron, so it won't ring. While she waits she looks at Ixx again and asks how many holes in the long room sleep light. She wants the slowest way through, not the fastest, because Bram's mail still talks a little even wrapped.

"Eleven holes." Ixx doesn't have to think about it. "Ixx counts them every day. Four sleep light." He puts the twigs of the broom down on the stone. "Slow way is Ixx's way. Round the four, wide. Before each one Ixx stops. When Ixx stops, you stop. When Ixx goes, you go." His eyes go to Bram's shoulders under the fleece. "By the four, rings don't talk."

Sefa takes her place for the line, hands bare and empty, with the Book quiet against her hip.

---

At the back wall Pell closes the purse over Dask's knees and works the stiff brass tongue back through its buckle. The twelve gold stay where the dead man carried them.

"New Amnian danters," he murmurs to nobody in particular. "Same minting, by the look of them."

He leaves the wrapped bundle in the closed fist and starts back up the aisle in the hobnail prints, lantern low, keeping wide of the chisel where it lies in its ring of thrown ash. Behind him the dark closes in shelf by shelf, until the room of burnt paper is black from end to end. A dead man sits somewhere in it with his hand shut on something nobody can see.

He stops in the doorway beside Sefa, without stepping out past Bram's shield, and speaks low to the two of them. He thinks the Warm One can't hear them in here. Before they answer him, they shouldn't let him hurry them into breaking what's left of that lock. They can hear him out at the door without promising anything.

Then he raises his voice just enough for the slope. "We heard you. Does the Warm One know Kesh gave us the fleeces? If he sends her against us, can she refuse?"

Ixx answers the first question at once. "He said walk soft. Iron quiet. Skins are iron quiet. Skins are what he wants." Kesh is in no trouble over the fleeces, as far as he can see.

The second question takes much longer. He looks down at the broom, then at the orange glow at the bottom of the slope.

"Kesh is eldest. Elders do what Kesh says." He stops. "Kesh does what he says."

He doesn't say anything for a while. When he does speak, it's quieter.

"Before Kesh, there was another eldest. When he came down. That one said no." He says nothing about what came after. His eyes stay on the glow.

"I don't want anyone hurt over his impatience," Pell says, level.

Ixx looks up at him. "Ixx neither."

Then he turns to face down the slope, sets the twigs to the groove in the floor, and stays still with the broom ready.

"Shut the black door," he says. "Then Ixx goes."

*(Out of game: the black door is still open, with Pell standing in it. Nobody has pulled it to yet, and Ixx won't start down until someone does.)*
````

## Round 19

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 19 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram moves half a step to one side without turning away from the slope, clearing the doorway so Pell can come out. He keeps his shield half-raised and his eyes on the lit wall below.

"Pell, pull it to with your hand wrapped. Bram speaks to you both: once that door is shut, we don't speak of Dellmere again until we're back above ground. No names below, not his, not Arren's unless the door already has it. From here to the lamp, we give him our faces and our terms, not our store of news."

He waits until he hears the door settle, then says to Ixx, "Now, Ixx. We step where you step. Slow at the four, wide around them. If you feel anything shift underfoot, you stop and we stop." He sets his boot into the space behind the kobold. "Lead."

Sefa Marrin: Sefa touches Pell's sleeve so he steps clear of the threshold, then leans in close to Bram's shoulder. She keeps it to a breath.

"The brass isn't on me. I left it in Dask's hand, wrapped. That room is the one place he can't see it, and carried down the slope it'd be the brightest thing in the cellar. Kesh said don't show it to him first. If the Book warms, it'll be from the lamp, not from anything in my belt." She waits half a beat. "I'll tell you everything once we're out."

Then she reaches past Pell and takes the green bronze ring in her bare hand. She lifts as she draws it, the way Pell did, so the foot doesn't drag, and eases the charred door shut. The hinges give their one low dry note. She keeps pulling until the gap is gone, so no cool air comes through, and lets the ring down onto the oak without a sound.

She lays her palm flat on the char for a moment, beside the dragged print, without quite touching it. *Keep it a little longer.*

She turns to Ixx.

"Shut. Go on, Ixx. Eleven holes, four light, round them wide. When you stop, we stop." She glances at his tail, still wound twice round his ankle. "And thank you for telling Pell about the other eldest. We'll keep it at words."

She falls in last, two steps behind Pell's lantern, hands bare and loose at her sides. She counts under her breath and watches where the broom's twigs touch, setting her boots in the same swept groove. Every time Ixx stops, she stops before her foot comes down. She doesn't look at the holes too long. She keeps her eyes on the broom and on Bram's wrapped shoulders. If a ring of his mail so much as whispers near one of the four, she'll touch Pell's back so the word goes forward without anyone raising a voice.

Pell Dunmore: Before closing it, I whisper to Bram and Sefa, “The brass is still wrapped in Dask’s hand. We left it there. Nothing from that room is coming with us.”

I ease the black door shut with my wrapped hand, lifting slightly so it doesn’t scrape, and let it settle without a slam.

“All right, Ixx. Lead us through.”

I take my place behind Bram, lantern low, following the broom’s path and keeping my padded gear clear of the walls.
````

**Card** · Bram Halloway · Stealth (Dexterity +1): Bram's wrapped mail past the four holes that sleep light. Ixx's slow lead cancels the chain mail's disadvantage · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram takes half a step sideways without turning from the slope. That clears the doorway behind him, and his shield stays half-raised toward the lit wall below. He gives the rule over his shoulder in a voice meant for two people. Once the door is shut nobody says Dellmere until they're above ground. No names below. Not that one, and not Arren's either unless the door already carries it. From here to the lamp the Warm One gets their faces and their terms, and nothing of what they've learned.

Sefa touches Pell's sleeve, and he steps back off the threshold to make room for her. She leans in at Bram's shoulder and keeps it to a breath. The brass isn't on her. It's in Dask's hand, wrapped, in the one room the Warm One can't see into. Carried down the slope it would be the brightest thing in the cellar, and Kesh said not to show it to him first. If the Book warms from here on, it's the lamp doing it and nothing in her belt. She'll tell him everything once they're out.

Then she reaches past Pell and takes the green bronze ring in her bare hand. She lifts as she draws, the way Pell did, so the foot of the door won't drag. The hinges give their one low dry note. She keeps pulling until the finger-wide gap is gone and no more cool air comes past her wrist, then lowers the ring onto the oak without a sound. For a moment her palm rests flat on the char beside the dragged print, close to it but not touching.

Pell's whisper reaches them just after the latch: the brass is still wrapped in Dask's hand, and nothing from that room comes with them. His wrapped hand had gone out toward the door. He lets it drop.

*(Out of game: Sefa's paragraph came first, so she shut the door. Pell finds it done.)*

With the door shut the passage closes back in. The hum returns to Bram's boot soles as if a mill-wheel had been put back in gear, and the heat leans on all three of them from below. Ten feet down the slope Ixx looks at the shut door, and something in his shoulders lets go.

"Shut," he says. When Sefa thanks him for telling Pell about the other eldest, his snout dips once. He doesn't look up. "Words," he says. "Good."

He turns, puts his twigs to the groove in the floor, and goes. *Shhk, shhk.*

Bram keeps to Ixx's line, slowly, with the hot wall a pace off his right shoulder. As he passes the bright niche he turns his shield toward it. He does it out of habit, putting the shield where the trouble is.

That's where the roll goes against him. Nobody wrapped the shield. Its iron rim swings in a short arc and taps the rings of his mail skirt below the fleece at his hip. It makes one small, clear *chink*, iron on iron, no louder than a coin dropped on a counter. In that room it carries.

The seam in the niche goes from dull red to orange to yellow in the space of a breath.

The shape uncurls. It's a little figure of cooled slag, knee-high to Bram. Its arms are too long and end in blunt stubs, and fire runs in its cracks the way water runs in a gutter. Two holes in its lumpy head glow like the mouths of a bellows. It doesn't climb out yet. It hunches on the lip of the niche, five feet from Bram's hip, with its head turned toward him, swinging slowly from side to side like a dog trying to place a sound. Heat comes off it in a wave that dries the sweat on his face.

Further along the wall, two other niches brighten. They don't wake. The sleepers in them turn over.

Ixx has stopped dead ten feet ahead with his broom lifted off the stone. He doesn't turn around. His voice comes back thin and fast, barely louder than the hum.

"Still. Stone still. He looks for the ring." A short breath. "Ring again, he comes. Ring again, they all come."

Behind Bram, Pell's lantern pool lies steady on the floor, and Sefa has stopped with her foot already down. The thing on the lip of the niche keeps turning its head, slowly, listening for the next sound.
````

## Round 20

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 20 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram goes stone-still. The shield stays where the tap happened, no effort made to pull it back, no breath big enough to stir it again. He lets the sweat run down the sides of his face. His eyes don't leave the little burning shape on the lip of the niche.

He hears Ixx and believes him. So he counts the creature's sway, and the four slow pulses of the vent, letting the silence drink his own warmth. One hand stays on his sword, but he doesn't coax the grip, doesn't twitch the leather—just the old blank patience of a man who's waited out a wolf on a dark road and knows movement is a language too.

When he's sure the creature hasn't found the sound's source, he moves only his head, a fraction, enough to put Sefa and Pell in the edge of his sight without turning from the niche. The word *hold* is already done, so he gives them his eyes, then his mouth shapes two more without sound: *when I move.*

If the thing turns away, toward the dark, he'll wait. If it comes off the lip, he'll put his shield between it and them, and then it'll be the shortest fight he knows how to make.

Sefa Marrin: Sefa goes still where she stands, mid-step, her weight on the foot already down. She doesn't speak, she doesn't touch Pell's back, and she doesn't let the Book shift at her hip. She breathes shallowly through the fleece, the way she used to on the ward when a fevered sleeper stirred and might wake screaming.

Her eyes stay on the slag-thing's swinging head, not on Bram. She keeps her right hand low and open at her side, fingers loose.

She doesn't cast. Eldritch Blast is light and force, no iron, but it would be noise of a kind, and she won't be the second sound. If it stays on its lip and settles, she does nothing at all. She waits for Ixx's broom to touch stone again before she so much as shifts her other foot.

*(Out of game: I'm readying an action. If the creature leaves the niche and goes for Bram, Sefa fires Eldritch Blast at it, aiming to use Repelling Blast to shove it back into its niche and away from him. If it doesn't move toward anyone, she holds and stays silent.)*

Pell Dunmore: I freeze with both feet planted, holding the lantern steady and clear of my gear. I don’t reach toward Bram or make another sound. I watch the slag creature without moving, waiting for it to settle or for Ixx’s signal.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Nobody moves.

Bram stays where the chink caught him, with the shield still hanging at the angle it swung to. He doesn't pull it back. Sweat runs from his hairline into the fleece at his collar and he lets it. His sword hand rests on the hilt and stays there. Five feet from his hip the slag-thing hunches on the lip of its niche, and its head keeps swinging, slow, from side to side.

Behind him Pell stands with both feet planted and the lantern held out from his body, so its bail can't touch anything. Sefa has stopped with her weight on the foot that came down. She breathes shallowly through the fleece, her right hand low and open at her side.

Ten feet ahead, Ixx holds his broom off the stone.

The head swings left, toward the hot wall and away from Bram, and stops there. The bellows-holes in its face flare once. Then it turns back the other way, slower, until those two glowing holes point at the fleece on Bram's hip. It leans out over the edge of the niche, a finger's width, then another. Bram feels the heat come off it onto his thigh through the wool and the rings, like standing too close to a forge mouth.

It sniffs. It's a dry, crackling draw of air, like a fire pulling at a chimney. Whatever it's looking for is iron that rings, and what it finds is wet goat.

The crack-light along its arms dims from yellow to orange. Its long arms draw in. It settles back onto the stone, folds itself down the way a dog turns round before it lies, and pulls its head in until the head is only a lump of slag among the others. The seam in the niche goes from yellow to orange to the dull red it was before. Further along the wall the two brightened niches dim with it, one and then the other, and the sleepers in them stay asleep.

Sefa never has to loose her blast. Nothing came off the lip.

Bram turns his head a fraction, just enough to get Sefa and Pell into the edge of his sight. He shapes two words with no sound behind them: *when I move.*

Ixx waits a long moment more with the broom held off the floor. Then he lets out a breath through his snout, so quietly it's barely there. He doesn't look round.

"Sleeps." It's thinner than a whisper. "Again light. Not deep."

He sets the twigs down on the stone with great care, beyond the cartwheel edge of the fourth hole, where the heat above the vent shimmers like water. *Shhk.* Then he takes one step on the far side of it and stops again, exactly where he said he would. He's waiting for the tall ones to go round the hole wide the way he did.

From where Bram stands he can see the rest of the long room in the orange light. Seven more holes lie between here and the far wall, and two of them are the light sleepers Ixx counted, one near the middle and one by the far arch. Past that arch the passage narrows and goes on down. At the end of it, small with the distance, there's a glow that isn't orange. It's paler, white-gold, about as bright as the top step at evening.

*(Out of game: the burning one settled back on its own. Your stillness gave it nothing to find, and no fight has opened.)*

 *(Out of game: neither readied action could go off. Both were waiting on the burning one leaving its niche, and it settled back asleep without coming off the lip, so Bram's shield never had to move and Sefa had no target for her blast.)*
````

## Round 21

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 21 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: You cross after Ixx, wide around the fourth hole, and the others follow. The long room gets hotter as you go. The hot breath out of the holes presses up through the fleeces and dries the last moisture from your lips. You pass the middle hole and the one by the far arch and none of the sleepers lifts its head. Twice a seam brightens behind you after you've passed, as if a sleeper has rolled over in the hot dark, and dims again. Ixx's broom and feet and breath are the only sounds ahead.

At last the flagstones narrow and slope down again. The arch at the far end is cut with the same little lantern, but bigger, and the white-gold light comes around the corner from beyond it in a slow tide, pulsing like a heartbeat you can feel through the stone.

Ixx stops at the near side of the arch, still ten feet out. He doesn't step under it.

"The door is there," he says. "The Warm One is behind. Ixx does not go closer. Ixx stays, sweeps." He says it flatly, so it won't be mistaken for a question, and his tail is wound round his ankle. He looks at you, then at Sefa, then back down the hallway behind you into the red dark of the long room. The white light touches the side of his face and shows how new the scorched edges of his apron are.

*(Out of game: at the near side of the arch you can see enough to place it. The white-gold light comes from somewhere to the left beyond the arch—perhaps a lamp beneath the smaller lantern-carving above it, perhaps the seam of a door. The sound from here is all furnace-heart: not loud, but deep and continuous, like a great bell left ringing beneath the floor.)*

Where you are: at the arch at the far end of the long room · Time: Day 1 · evening

Sefa Marrin: Sefa lets the breath out slowly through the fleece and doesn't move until Bram does. Then she follows his line exactly: the broom's line, wide round the shimmering edge of the fourth hole. She sets each boot down heel first and slow, so the studs on her leather don't creak. As she passes the niche she doesn't look straight at the lump of slag. She watches it from the corner of her eye, the way you watch a sleeper you don't want to feel watched.

She keeps her right hand low and open the whole way. Nothing raised, nothing cast.

Once they're past the four light sleepers, with the dull red niches behind them and Ixx stopped at his next mark, she closes the two steps to Pell and reaches past him to touch Bram's elbow, once, lightly. She tugs free the fold of fleece she'd tucked under her belt, the one covering the worst of her studs, and presses it into his hand. In the barest breath, she nods at the shield's rim.

"Edge. Wrap it, or keep it flat to you. I'll live with a few studs."

She takes her place at the back again, the uncovered studs at her waist now held still under her left palm as she walks. She keeps her eyes on the broom.

When Ixx stops, she stops. When he goes, she goes. She's still counting under her breath, now the holes as well as the steps, so she'll know them again on the way back up.

Pell Dunmore: I wait for Bram to move, then follow his path around the hole, wide as Ixx showed us. I keep the lantern out from my gear and give Bram enough room that neither of us can brush the other. No words—just slow, careful steps.
````

**The DM's narration:**

````text
The burning one in the fourth niche is a lump of dull slag again, its seam back to banked red. Ixx waits on the far side of the shimmer with his broom held off the stone.

Bram goes round the hole the way Ixx went, wide, keeping to the edge of the heat. He holds the shield flat and still against his body and doesn't let it swing toward the niche this time. The air over the vent ripples like water over a weir and comes up under his chin hot enough to make his eyes sting. He takes it slowly, one foot down, weight on, then the next. The mail rustles under the wool, and nothing chinks.

Pell comes after him, a long stride back so the two of them can't brush, and holds the lantern out from his body on a straight arm. He doesn't make a sound. Hardly anyone on the Wide would have heard him.

Sefa comes last, setting each boot down heel first. She keeps her eyes on the broom and watches the niche only from the corner of her eye. The lump of slag doesn't stir as she passes.

Ixx lets them close up behind him. Then he puts the twigs down and moves on, *shhk*, about twenty feet, and stops at his next mark. It's well short of the middle hole, a broad mouth in the floor with heat standing over it. To its left a niche glows the dull red of a coal that hasn't quite gone out. That's one of the light sleepers. The far arch is beyond it, with the last one beside it, and past that the white-gold pulses slow and steady.

While they wait at the mark, Sefa closes the two steps to Pell and reaches past him to touch Bram's elbow, just once. She pulls the fold of fleece from under her belt, the piece that was covering the worst of her studs, and presses it into his free hand. She nods at the iron rim of his shield and breathes the words so quietly the hum nearly swallows them.

"Edge. Wrap it, or keep it flat to you. I'll live with a few studs."

She drops back to her place at the end of the line with her left palm flat over the bare studs at her waist.

Up ahead, Ixx has turned his head. He sees the fleece go from her hand to Bram's, and his eyes go to the shield rim. He doesn't speak and he doesn't move off yet. He's waiting, with the twigs resting on the stone, for the tall ones to be ready.

Bram is standing in the heat with a loose fold of rank goat wool in his hand, and the middle sleeper's niche glows twenty feet ahead.

*(Out of game: Bram's paragraph went on to the far arch and what Ixx says there. You're not there yet. Getting past the last two light sleepers will be a Stealth check for Bram, and what he does with the fleece in his hand is his call.)*

 *(Out of game: the crossing past the middle and far-arch sleepers, and the Stealth check that goes with it, are waiting because Sefa just handed Bram the fleece and gave him a choice: wrap the rim or keep the shield flat. That choice changes the roll, so the turn stopped there for Bram's player.)*

*(Out of game: Bram's player decides what he does with the fleece. Once that's settled, I'll roll the Stealth check and play the rest of the crossing.)*
````
