## Round 22

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 22 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram takes the fleece and tucks it over the iron rim of the shield with short, hard tucks, working it down until the edge is covered all the way round. He turns the shield flat to his side and pulls his elbow in so the wool doesn't bunch at his hip. He tests it once—no ring, only the dull hush of fleece on fleece.

Then he meets Ixx's eye and gives him one slow nod: ready.

He doesn't look back at Sefa, but he says it too low to carry past her ear: "Stay in my lee. If the middle one wakes, don't scatter—fall back on the mark we left."

What he means is: to the last breathing mark, back up the room. He'll hold the line there.

Sefa Marrin: Sefa doesn't say anything more. She's given Bram the fleece, and what he does with it is his call. She stays where she stopped, two steps behind Pell, her left palm flat over the bare studs at her waist and her right hand loose at her side.

If Bram moves to wrap the rim, she steps up to his shield side. She doesn't take the shield. She just holds out her open hands, low, so he can rest its edge on her palms instead of the floor while he winds the wool and ties it off. Leather and skin don't ring. If he shakes his head or keeps the shield flat to his body, she steps back to her place without a word.

While she waits she keeps counting under her breath, steps and holes together. *Five from the black door to the first. Fourth light, by the bright niche. Middle one next, light, on the left.* She marks the dull red glow of the middle sleeper's niche and the last one by the far arch, so she'll know them on the way back up even without a broom in front of her.

She doesn't look at the white-gold beyond the arch for long. Once, briefly, to see it. Then her eyes go back to Ixx's twigs on the stone.

When Bram is ready and Ixx moves, she moves. She sets her heel down first, slowly, and goes wide where the broom goes wide.

Pell Dunmore: I stand still while Bram pads the shield, keeping the lantern clear and its light low on his hands. I watch the nearest niche for any brightening and wait for Ixx to lead us on.
````

**Card** · Bram Halloway · Stealth, Dexterity +1: getting Bram's mail and shield past the two last light sleepers, the middle hole and the one by the far arch. Chain mail gives disadvantage; Ixx's known path and every iron edge wrapped give advantage; they cancel. · roll · presses: [Roll the die]

**Card** · Bram Halloway · Stealth, Dexterity +1: getting Bram's mail and shield past the two last light sleepers, the middle hole and the one by the far arch. Chain mail gives disadvantage; Ixx's known path and every iron edge wrapped give advantage; they cancel. · decide · presses: prose, prose, [Onward]

**The DM's narration:**

````text
Bram takes the fold of fleece. Before he can lower the shield to the floor, Sefa steps up on his shield side and holds out both hands low, palms up. He looks at them for half a breath, then sets the iron rim down on her palms instead of on the stone. Leather and skin take its weight without a sound.

He works fast. He pulls the wool over the rim in short, hard tucks, a hand's width at a time, all the way round, and works the fleece down into the gap between rim and oak so it can't ride up. He has no gut left to tie it with, so he jams the last fold under the grip-strap and pulls the strap tight on top of it. Pell keeps the lantern clear and low the whole time, its light on Bram's hands and nowhere else, and watches the middle niche over his shoulder. The dull red glow in the niche doesn't change.

Bram lifts the shield off Sefa's palms, turns it flat against his side and pulls his elbow in. He tests it once against his hip. Fleece meets fleece with a soft hush, and nothing rings.

Sefa goes back to her place two steps behind Pell. Her left palm is back over the bare studs at her waist, and her lips move with her count: holes and steps, the fourth by the bright niche, the middle one next on the left.

Bram gives Ixx one slow nod. Without turning, he breathes to Sefa, low enough that it stops at her ear: "Stay in my lee. If the middle one wakes, don't scatter. Fall back on the mark we left."

Ixx's eyes go over the wrapped rim once. Then he sets the twigs down. *Shhk.*

He leads them left of the middle hole, close under the glowing wall and wide of the heat that stands over the vent. The niche of the middle sleeper is right there at shoulder height, an arm's length off Bram's shield side.

Bram gets three steps past the mouth of the middle niche, and it's the mail that gives him away, not the shield. His sword arm is the one part of him the fleeces don't cover. As he draws his elbow in to keep the shield flat, the rings of his sleeve slide over the rings at his ribs with a short dry rasp. It's iron grinding on iron, quieter than the chink at the fourth hole, but it happens right beside a sleeper that sleeps light.

The niche flares. It goes from dull red to yellow with nothing in between.

This one doesn't stop on the lip to listen. It uncurls and comes out at once, all its too-long arms first, and drops to the floor between Bram and the wall with a soft crunch, like a cinder landing on a hearth. It's the same kind of thing as the first, knee-high cooled slag with fire running in its cracks, and it's close enough now that Bram can feel its heat on his shin through his boot. Its two bellows-holes turn up to him. It sniffs, and this time it doesn't find only goat. Its head swings past the fleece at his hip and stops at his bare sword arm, and the light in its cracks goes from yellow toward white.

It lifts one blunt stub and reaches toward his elbow. It hasn't touched him yet. Where the heat off it leans against the greasy fleece at his hip, the wool starts to curl and smoke, and the smell of scorched goat fills the space between them.

Behind them, back by the fourth hole, the bright niche brightens again, though nothing comes out of it. Ahead, by the far arch, the last sleeper's niche goes from red to orange and stays there.

Ten feet ahead, Ixx has turned all the way round this time with the broom held off the stone. His voice comes thin and fast.

"Wool burns. Grease burns." His eyes go to the smoking fleece and then to the stub reaching for Bram's arm. "He wants the iron under. He touches, iron gets hot, *you* burn."

The burning one's stub hangs a hand's width from Bram's elbow, and the fleece at his hip has started to smoke. Pell is right behind Bram with the lantern, and Sefa is two steps behind Pell. None of you has moved yet.
````

## Round 23

### Scene: Bram Halloway, Sefa Marrin, Pell Dunmore (order: Bram, Sefa, Pell)

**What the DM was sent:**

````text
(Round 23 — one paragraph per character in this scene.)

Bram Halloway: Bram doesn't flinch from the reaching stub. He lets it come as far as a hand's width from the iron sleeve, then moves all at once and without violence: he yanks the smoking fleece from his hip with his shield hand and drops it square over the creature's arm. The greasy wool lands wet and hissing, and the thing jerks back a half-step, shaking it like a burning rag.

In the same breath he turns his sword arm behind his back, away from the bellows-holes, and sets his wrapped shield between it and the creature.

"Walk, don't run. To the arch." The words are low and even, less than a whisper, meant for Pell and Sefa. "Stay behind me. Don't touch the walls and don't knock your steel."

He doesn't draw. To draw would be to ring the scabbard and offer a hotter iron to the whole room. He backs one slow pace toward the far arch, keeping the shield low and flat, his eyes on the burning one's face the way he'd watch a dog that has been kicked before.

Sefa Marrin: Sefa doesn't wait for the stub to land. The burning one has made its choice, so the time for keeping still is over.

She hisses one word forward, sharp and low: "*Bram, back.*" She sidesteps a half pace to her left so Pell's shoulder isn't in her line, and brings her right hand up, open, toward the slag-thing at Bram's elbow.

The Lantern's light gathers in her palm and goes out in two lances, aimed low, at the thing's body and not near Bram's arm. The first beam is meant to *shove* it, to knock it off him and away along the wall, past its niche and out of reach of his iron. The second follows it wherever the first beam sent it.

As she looses she says, quick and level, "Pell, keep the lantern clear and stay behind me. Ixx, get away from the wall. If it bursts when it breaks, it burns."

Her left hand is already on the Book. If Bram takes fire, she means to send the Lantern's warmth after him in the same breath.

*(Out of game: Eldritch Blast, both beams at the burning one, with Repelling Blast on the first hit to push it 10 feet straight away from Bram, along the wall and away from the party. I know it may be a magmin-type that bursts when it dies, and that the noise may wake the others. I'd rather it broke 10 feet from Bram than burned him from the elbow. If he's hit or set alight, my bonus action is Healing Light on Bram, 2d6.)*

Pell Dunmore: “Bram—forward, slowly. Don’t strike it.”

Keeping the lantern out and away from the smoking wool, I reach my wrapped hand toward the fleece at Bram’s back to guide him forward along Ixx’s swept path, away from the reaching stub. I don’t tug his mail or step toward the vent.

“Ixx, show us the safe way clear.”
````
